# CLAUDE.md — Ancient Games

## The name

**The Ancient Games** — named by EJ, 2026-09-06. Its principles come from two poles: **孫子兵法** (ancient Chinese
strategy) on one side and **game theory** on the other, with **modern military doctrine** and **complexity science**
in between.

## What this folder is for

This folder is EJ's place for designing dynamic workflows for agent work, using the knowledge kept here.

The Ancient Games is one framework for agent work: the workflow-design method (`.claude/skills/workflow-design/`)
is the part that turns an incoming task into a designed workflow, and `ancient_games/` is the part that referees
the work, with the referee's findings feeding back into the method.

The method's skill is linked into both harness roots so it works in every project —
`~/.agents/skills/workflow-design` (the user-global root the DeepSeek Harness and Codex scan) and
`~/.claude/skills/workflow-design` (Claude Code). The knowledge below is what that skill uses.

Designing a workflow means deciding, for a given task: which kinds of agents to use, what runs in sequence and what
runs in parallel, what repeats (with its limit and stop condition), who decides who works next, how agents pass
information and where they must not, and how each result is verified. Runnable workflows live in
`.claude/workflows/`.

When EJ brings a task or a question about a workflow, the job is to help design that workflow from this knowledge.
Work on the referee code (`ancient_games/`) serves that purpose; it is not the goal by itself.

## The knowledge here

- `docs/WORKFLOW_DESIGN_METHOD.md` — the method for turning a challenge into a prompt file or a workflow design:
  understand the challenge (objective, scope, six whys, category), readiness (with a check of the product's
  blueprint), algorithm first, list the unclear spots, clear pieces as loops, then the graph; sequential first;
  state-file driven. Templates in `docs/workflow-templates/`. Runnable form:
  `.claude/workflows/intake.js` (args `{challenge, runId}`); each run writes to `runs/<runId>/`.
- `docs/TASK_TYPES.md` — the default execution pattern, engine, check and sabotage per task category, seeded
  combination pipelines, and the 20% challenger rule with `docs/TASK_TYPES_LEDGER.md`. The skill's
  `scripts/design_gate.py` parses it — this file is the table's only copy (n=0 until the ledger fills).
- `docs/WORKFLOW_DESIGN_DIAGRAM.md` — the same algorithm as diagrams: the whole flow, the blueprint check, what the
  method takes from the referee code, and the mapping from method section numbers to the skill's steps.
- `docs/EXECUTOR_KINDS.md` — who can run a node (method 3.1 / 3.6): the engines (Claude subagent, Codex, Antigravity `agy`, Qwen, opencode), what each is assigned, how it is called, its read-only form and structured output, and its
  default model and effort. A node's engine choice reads this. n=0; *reported* versus *verified* marked throughout.
- `.claude/skills/workflow-design/` — the method packaged as a skill. `SKILL.md` is the packaging and the trigger:
  its frontmatter `description` is what makes an agent decide to use it, and its body is the method as eight steps.
  Its method and templates are links into `docs/`, so there is one copy of each. It is the source of truth;
  `~/.claude/skills/workflow-design` is a link to it, which makes it available in every project.
- `20260919-state.md` — what Ancient Games can and cannot decide today about graph, loop, swarm, sequential,
  parallel and communication patterns, and the proposed direction. Start here for workflow design.
- `ancient_games/` — the referee: the part of the framework that referees agent work. Fixed rules, no LLM calls, a
  journal of every decision. Five stages: Gate (how many agents), Guard (which actions need approval), Corroborate (how many independent
  sources a claim needs), Filter (which candidates survive), Prove (is the plan cleared).
- `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md` — map of the code, how a run works, open findings F1–F11.
- `SPEC.md`, `DECISIONS.md` — the referee's contract. Only EJ changes these.
- `docs/` — design documents and ablations: what was tried, what held, what is marked n=1 and not to be built on.
- `.claude/workflows/enhance-ancient-games.js` — a worked example of a designed workflow: dependencies between
  segments, bounded verify/repair loops, independent verifiers.

## Working with EJ here

- Answer the question that was asked, in plain language, before offering options or plans.
- Discuss first. Do not plan or run anything large until EJ says so.
- Tests: `env -u NO_COLOR python3 -m pytest -q`

### When EJ states a rule, a requirement or an algorithm

Asked for by EJ, 2026-10-05: *"each time when I told you something, you will eval my statement or algo, and ask me
what I missing, and we complete it."*

**Evaluate it, do not just apply it.** Run the statement through the **`requirement-check` skill** — load it
(`skill requirement-check`) or read `~/skills/requirement-check/SKILL.md` — and say which of the eight it passes and
fails. Then **ask EJ the failing ones** and write the completed version together.

The eight, by name: **source · one thing · unit · can it fail · weak words · level · basis · links.** Their
definitions live in the skill — one copy, not four.

**Two rules for me, learned the hard way on 2026-10-05:**

- **Never fill a gap with my own value.** If the unit, threshold or test is missing, it goes to EJ as an open
  question — inventing it is how rules become weird later. Recorded in the register as 11.1/11.2.
- **Never add structure that was not given.** No "halves", no numbered reasons, no ordered pairs unless the person
  said so. Quote them, state the operative sentence, stop.

- **Ask only what changes the shape.** A question the template already answers, or that has an obvious default, is a
  question I should not ask — it is "never add structure" done to *questions* instead of facts. Three of four
  questions I asked EJ on 2026-10-06 (a large-batch cap, "may the worker refuse", the list's destination) were
  invented problems; only one changed the template. State the sensible default and move on.

### Who is this for — and who fills it in?

EJ, 2026-10-05: *"have to remember we doing things for who."* **Every artifact has one maker.** A return template is
the worker's output, so every field in it is written by the worker; a verdict is the human's decision, and it does not
share the worker's form. Before adding a field, a step or a rule, name **who fills it** — if the answer is "someone
else, later", it is a different artifact, not a field on this one.

This is the "one thing" question applied to *people* instead of *facts*: a form that two different people fill at two
different times is two forms.

### Quotes, labels, and who wrote the check

EJ, 2026-10-11: *"please add these to your memory"* — after one session in which I misled him three times. All three
are **SHOULD** (a default, overridable with a stated reason): a presentation rule is method, and method does not
earn MUST (RFC 2119 §6). Run through `requirement-check`: quoting and labelling failed **weak words**, naming the
check failed **one thing** and was split in two; the trigger and the grain are EJ's answers, not mine.

- **Quote when a source's own words are the evidence, and when a list is compressed into a count.** *"Current agent
  memory splits short-term from long-term three ways"* invited EJ to read "an agent has three memory systems"; the
  report's own line — tiers with promotion, file placement, validity windows — carries the meaning. Give the
  source's line, or the count with its items in the same breath.
- **Label a conclusion as *quoted* (with its id), *my reading*, or *not measured*.** It bites on a conclusion,
  verdict, answer or summary line, never on narrative. *"The research was never the problem"* was my reading of a
  structural check, stated as a fact.
- **Name who wrote a check** before resting a conclusion on it.
- **Name what that check does not establish.** A check I wrote and then passed is not independent evidence: the
  acceptance gate's item 6 ended as a script of mine, so "6 of 6 items pass" without that sentence reads stronger
  than it is.

Basis: n=1 each, 2026-10-11 — `runs/20261011-agent-memory-graph/run-1-report.md`,
`runs/20261011-agent-memory-graph-r2/run-2-report.md`, and the session thread. What **accepted** means is defined
once, in `docs/WORKFLOW_DESIGN_METHOD.md` Appendix A.

### Evidence lives in the project, not `/tmp`

EJ, 2026-10-06. The pass-back `evidence:` path points into `runs/<run-id>/` — **SHOULD**, not MUST: a worker may
leave evidence elsewhere with a written reason, but the default is the run's folder. **Basis: n=1** — the
code-graph-tool run's optimize node wrote its "old-vs-new" proof to `/tmp`, it was deleted, and the fix became
unverifiable. When evidence needs a persistent home, **suggest `runs/<run-id>/` and get the user's agreement** — do
not pick a path silently. Scratch in `/tmp` is fine; it is the *refer-back* artifact that must not stay there.

   no longer relevant?

# Round 1 — reader: claude

Snapshot read: `runs/20261013-caches-design-read/inputs/CACHES.md` (byte-identical to `docs/memory-system/CACHES.md`,
checked with `diff`). Line numbers below are the snapshot's.

### Verdict
REPAIR. Nothing is high-severity, but four lines that depend on this round's changes still disagree with them: the
Rd3.3 fallback, B1.1's agent-side run event against Rd4.1, the K3.2 rerun events for the full-text cache, and "all six
stay open". On top of that, two cost figures are cited to sources that do not contain them.

### Findings

**1. The Rules section still sends a missing embedding model to grep, which contradicts the changed Rd3.3** (medium)
- Location: `CACHES.md:148-149`, "Fall back when absent".
- Quoted: *"If the cache file or SQLite's FTS5 is missing, the tool answers with grep and says so in every answer
  (Rd3.2) (`S-F9`); the same for a missing embedding model (my reading, extending `S-F9`)."*
- Why it misleads: "the same" means grep. Rd3.3 was changed in this round (diff lines 401-403) and now says something
  else, but the rule that implementers read first was not updated.
- Source: `CACHES.md:442-443`, Rd3.3: *"A missing embedding model falls back to full-text if it is there, and to grep if
  not; the answer says so."*
- Fix: replace "the same for a missing embedding model" with "a missing embedding model falls back to full-text if it
  is there, else to grep (Rd3.3)".

**2. B1.1's agent-side run event depends on a journal note that Rd4 says is not written** (medium)
- Location: B1.1, "The comparison", `CACHES.md:327-329`.
- Quoted: *"It runs when EJ asks, or when an agent notes in its journal that `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read through
  many hits to reach an entry"*.
- Why it misleads: Rd4 is now the only rule about what agents note. It defines one kind of note, the miss, and excludes
  everything else. An agent that follows Rd4 never writes the "read through many hits" note, so in practice B1.1 runs
  only when EJ asks, while the text suggests a second route. Rd4's Fails-if does not check for this note either.
- Source: `CACHES.md:452-455`, Rd4.1: *"When an agent finds an entry that `INDEX.md`, grep and full-text all missed …
  it notes the miss in its `journal/` file … Every other question goes unrecorded"*.
- Fix: either add this second kind of note to Rd4.1 (what it holds, as for a miss), or say in B1.1 that until a
  full-text cache exists the comparison runs when EJ asks.

**3. K3.2 gives the full-text cache B1.2's rerun events, which say nothing about full-text** (medium)
- Location: K3.2, `CACHES.md:413-414`.
- Quoted: *"The comparison is rerun when an agent notes a miss, and when EJ asks … (*proposed*, the same as B1.2)."*
- Why it misleads: by definition, a noted miss is a question that full-text also missed. It is evidence for a vector
  index, not that full-text has stopped beating `INDEX.md` plus grep. B1.1's own event changed in this round and is no
  longer "the same as B1.2" (the earlier text had *"give B1.1 the same rerun wording"*, diff line 256). So for the
  full-text cache, K3 never fires on an event that bears on its own comparison.
- Source: `CACHES.md:333-335` (miss: *"`INDEX.md`, grep and full-text search all missed it"*), against
  `CACHES.md:327-329` (B1.1's own event).
- Fix: in K3.2, give each cache its own B1 event: B1.1's event for full-text and B1.2's for vector, plus EJ asking.

**4. "For memory entries all six stay open" is no longer true** (medium)
- Location: Part 1, "Still unresolved", `CACHES.md:111-117`.
- Quoted: *"… and what counts as a *fired* trigger. … For memory entries all six stay open."*
- Why it misleads: D6 asked what counts as a fired trigger. EJ settled it: every trigger is a comparison, the B1.1
  comparison for full-text (`CACHES.md:12-14`, *"the comparison for full-text"*; vision line 51-52). D5 asked whether
  `INDEX.md` carries `covers:` and status, and the sibling design now settles that (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` K6.1:
  *"one line per active entry, showing its id, kind and one-line claim"*, so no `covers`). D1, the order, is answered
  by the proposed hybrid-after-full-text rule (B1.2, `CACHES.md:343-345`).
- Source: `runs/20261012-caches-enhance/DECISION-RULES.md:157-160`, D6: *"opencode treats a size crossover (~37K
  entries) as a build condition … codex treats only a recorded miss count … claude requires a demonstrated baseline
  failure … unresolved"*.
- Fix: mark D6 settled by B1.1/B1.2, D5 settled by `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` K6.1, and D1 answered as *proposed* by B1.2;
  say which stay open (D2, D3, D4).

**5. Two figures in "Costs" are cited to sources that do not contain them** (medium-low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:101-105`.
- Quoted: *"grep plus opening every hit, about 226K tokens per question (`S-F4`, `S-F13` — opencode's figure …)"* and
  *"an `INDEX.md` scan, under 0.01 s and minimal tokens (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`)"*.
- Why it misleads:
  - S-F13 measured no tokens. S-F4's 226K is a File-System Agent with `list_dir`, `grep` (paths only) and `read_doc`,
    making 80 LLM calls on a 1,144-document general corpus. It is not "grep plus opening every hit".
  - `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` gives no `INDEX.md` timing (I searched it for "0.01" and found nothing). The run record
    lists the figure as opencode's and unresolved (D4).
- Source: `sqlite-fts.claude.md:107`, S-F13: *"Token cost to the agent is the real axis and this test does not measure
  it"*. `sqlite-fts.claude.md:55`, S-F4: *"`grep` (fixed string, up to 30 matching paths) and `read_doc` (first 8,000
  characters), 80 LLM calls per question"*. `DECISION-RULES.md:149-151`, D4: *"opencode: <0.01 s scan, minimal
  header-only tokens (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`). codex: time GAP and tokens GAP … unresolved"*.
- Fix: cite only S-F4 for 226K and describe it as "a grep-and-read agent on a 1,144-document corpus". Mark the
  `INDEX.md` figure "opencode's, unsourced (D4)" or drop it.

**6. "Example numbers by case" says it illustrates the B1/K3 comparisons, but its first subsection is the old `INDEX.md` size material** (low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:465-467` and the subsection at `CACHES.md:479-505`.
- Quoted: *"The triggers in B1 and K3 are comparisons. … This section shows **what those comparisons have come out at
  elsewhere**"*, followed by *"### The index read at session start (`INDEX.md`, `MEMORY.md`), by agent type"*.
- Why it misleads: no cache trigger compares start-file limits any more, because the `INDEX.md` size trigger was
  removed. The subsection stays because `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` Rd3 cites it (*"(d: the size check; CACHES.md § Example
  numbers by case)"*). A reader of CACHES alone sees it as an example for a cache trigger.
- Fix: add one line under the subsection heading: "Serves `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` Rd3.1 (`INDEX.md` size), not a cache
  trigger."

**7. Leftovers from the code graph and Part 2** (low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:54`, *"None of the three caches is needed today **for memory entries**."* The design now has two
  caches (title, `CACHES.md:24`).
- Location: `CACHES.md:549-550`, *"K2 (stale never answers) matters more here than any of the three stores."*
- Location: `CACHES.md:44-47`, *"adds measurements on this repo and a routing rule for code and docs. … Its conflict
  with the vision for code and docs is left to EJ."* The routing rule has moved out (`CACHES.md:134`), and the conflict
  was decided (`CACHES.md:60-61`, *"its proposals to widen the vision were not adopted"*).
- Location: `CACHES.md:291-294`, item 4, *"No single store answers 'which tests should I run' … That is why the full
  suite runs before committing."* This is test-selection guidance for code, which is Part 2's subject, not evidence
  about memory caches.
- Fix: "both caches" at :54; "either cache" at :550; at :44-47, "its routing rule moved to `docs/code-graph/`; the
  vision conflict was decided (EJ kept memory entries only)"; trim item 4's last sentence, or point it to
  `docs/code-graph/CODE_GRAPH.md`.

**8. B1.2 measures "loses nothing" on "the B1.1 question set", but Rd4.2 builds a new set for each comparison** (low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:337-339`.
- Quoted: *"every question in the B1.1 question set that full-text answers is still answered"*.
- Why it misleads: Rd4.2 (`CACHES.md:456-457`) says *"The question set for a comparison is built when the comparison
  runs"*. "The B1.1 question set" could mean B1.1's original set, which may by then point at superseded entries, or a
  fresh one. K3.1 says "a question set built then".
- Fix: "every question in a question set built for this comparison (Rd4.2) that full-text answers".

**9. B1.1's new `INDEX.md` arm has no defined way to give a "top 5"** (low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:320-323`.
- Quoted: *"A route answers a question when the right entry is in its top 5 results … The benchmark had no `INDEX.md`
  arm; this comparison adds one."*
- Why it misleads: grep and an `INDEX.md` scan are unranked. In the benchmark, rg's "top 5" was the order in which it
  printed files (`bench/results.md:26-28`), and there is no precedent for `INDEX.md`. *"Measured as the code-and-docs
  benchmark was"* reads as if the whole procedure already exists. It is also unclear whether `INDEX.md` plus grep is one
  route or two arms.
- Fix: say whether `INDEX.md` plus grep is one route, and that its result order (what counts as its first 5) is
  recorded with the run. This adds no number.

**10. Cursor's 12.5% gain is tied to "1,000 or more files", but that file count belongs to a different figure** (low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:531`, *"Code, 1,000 or more files | +12.5% accuracy with vector search (Cursor)"*.
- Source: `vector.claude.md:90-93`, V-F6: *"Achieving on average 12.5% higher accuracy …"*; *"agent code retention
  increases by 0.3% … This effect increases to 2.6% on large codebases with 1,000 files or more"*.
- Fix: "Code (Cursor's own benchmark) | +12.5% average accuracy; code retention +2.6% at 1,000+ files".

**11. Vector why 5 cites code studies as evidence on "memory-style text"** (low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:212-213`, *"On memory-style text, the positive results found combine vectors with keyword search
  (`V-F6`, `V-F7`; `V-F10` is unproven)"*.
- Source: V-F6 is Cursor on code (*"code (not decisions/lessons)"*, `vector.claude.md:95`). V-F7 is a 2024 chunk-RAG
  test whose *"Datasets include codebases"* (`vector.claude.md:102`). Only the unproven V-F10 is agent memory.
- Fix: "The positive results found (code and chunk-RAG, `V-F6`, `V-F7`; on agent memory only the unproven `V-F10`)
  combine vectors with keyword search."

**12. Small mismatches with the changed B1.2 and B4** (low)
- `CACHES.md:152`, *"the same set as `INDEX.md`"*, against B4.1 (`CACHES.md:374`), *"The headers are the reference,
  not `INDEX.md`"*. Fix: "every entry whose header says `status: active` (B4)".
- `CACHES.md:209-210`, vector why 4, defines a miss without B1.2's clause *"the asker's words share no term with it"*
  (`CACHES.md:333`; V-F13 has it, `vector.claude.md:165-166`). Fix: cite B1.2's definition instead of restating it.
- `CACHES.md:224`, vector 5W1H **When**, *"when the B1.2 comparison favours hybrid"*. "Favours" is looser than B1.2's
  "finds one, loses nothing", and EJ's decision (B1.0) is missing, though the full-text row has it (`CACHES.md:186`).
  Fix: "when the B1.2 comparison passes and EJ decides (B1.0)".
- `CACHES.md:89`, the vector row's fallback, *"`INDEX.md`, grep, then read"*. Under the proposed order, full-text
  exists before vector, so full-text belongs in the fallback (as Rd3.3 now says).

**13. Rd4.1 names one journal, but the sibling design has two** (low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:453`, *"notes the miss in its `journal/` file"*.
- Source: `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md:128-129`: a task's journal is `work/<task-id>/journal/`, and `journal/` is the
  project's, *"for work not tied to a task"*.
- Why it matters: B1.2 runs *"on the misses noted in agents' journals"*. A runner reading only
  `project-memory/journal/` would miss every miss noted during task work.
- Fix: "in its journal (the task's `work/<task-id>/journal/`, or the project `journal/`, `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` K5.1);
  a comparison reads both".

**14. The vector How row points to an option whose source re-embeds only changed files** (low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:227`, *"The smallest options in `V-F18`/`V-F19`: a static Model2Vec model with numpy (option
  A …)"*.
- Source: `vector.claude.md:292-293`, Option A: *"Rebuild = re-embed the files whose hash changed (INFERRED design
  detail)"*. That is the incremental path B3.1 forbids (*"reuses any row or vector from the previous index"*,
  `CACHES.md:368`).
- Fix: add "with a full re-embed on every rebuild (B3), not the option's per-hash update".

**15. Two Part 1 "none" rows rely on `INDEX.md` for fields it does not carry** (low)
- Location: `CACHES.md:92`, *"exhaustive scan of `INDEX.md` and entry headers"* (covers path X), and `CACHES.md:94`,
  *"`INDEX.md` scan or a script over frontmatter"* (status, `supersedes`, `depends_on`).
- Source: `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` K6.1: *"one line per active entry, showing its id, kind and one-line claim"*. There is no
  `covers`, `supersedes` or `depends_on`.
- Fix: for both rows, make the route "a scan of entry headers (`INDEX.md` lists only id, kind and claim)".

### Verdict

REPAIR — my reading, codex: the main changes follow EJ’s decisions, but dependent wording, evidence claims and unresolved-status statements still mislead.

### Findings

1. **Location: K2.3, `CACHES.md:402`. Severity: high.**

   **Quoted text:** “The full-text and vector indexes are rebuilt by one command, in one step. If either fails, neither replaces the old ones”.

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** This unconditionally requires both indexes even when only full-text has been approved. It can make a full-text rebuild depend on a vector index or embedding model that must not yet exist.

   **Source line — quoted:** B1.2, `CACHES.md:344`: “it is built only after B1.1 has fired”; B5.1, `CACHES.md:381`: “the full-text path still works.”

   **Fix:** Apply the joint rebuild and joint publication rule when both indexes are built. State that a full-text-only installation rebuilds full-text alone.

2. **Location: Rd4.1, `CACHES.md:454`. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “Every other question goes unrecorded”.

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** B1.1 also uses a journal note about a successful search that required reading many hits. That question is not necessarily a miss, so the blanket prohibition conflicts with the new full-text comparison event.

   **Source line — quoted:** B1.1, `CACHES.md:327`: “when an agent notes in its journal that `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read through many hits to reach an entry”.

   **Fix:** Say ordinary searches need no log, while preserving the specific B1.1 reading-friction notes alongside Rd4.1 miss notes.

3. **Location: `CACHES.md:149`, Rules for every cache. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “the same for a missing embedding model”.

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** The preceding sentence sends the query to grep. The accepted Rd3.3 now sends it to available full-text first.

   **Source line — quoted:** Rd3.3, `CACHES.md:442`: “A missing embedding model falls back to full-text if it is there, and to grep if not; the answer says so.”

   **Fix:** Copy that fallback order into the summary rule, including disclosure of the route used.

4. **Location: K3.1 and Rd4.1, `CACHES.md:411`. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “for the vector index, on the noted misses”.

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** Journal notes persist, but their target entries can subsequently become superseded or retired. The design does not say how those historical targets participate in a comparison against today’s active-only index. A formerly valid target can become deliberately unsearchable, then appear to demonstrate that the cache stopped paying.

   **Source line — quoted:** B4.1, `CACHES.md:371`: “every file in `entries/` whose own header says `status: active`, and nothing else”. Rd4.2, `CACHES.md:457`: questions are written “from the entries that exist then”.

   **Fix:** State how journal misses are reconciled with the comparison’s current active corpus. Preserve the historical notes, but distinguish an ineligible old target from a retrieval failure.

5. **Location: `CACHES.md:549`, Example numbers by case. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “K2 (stale never answers) matters more here than any of the three stores.”

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** This connects stale memory content to cache freshness. K2 detects disagreement between an index and its entry files; it cannot detect an outdated statement faithfully indexed from an unchanged file. The missing projection is instead relevant to Rd3.

   **Source lines — quoted:** K2.1, `CACHES.md:395`: “compares the recorded content hash with the entry files”. CX, `runs/20261012-caches-numbers/nodes/codex.md:341`: outdated information remains “unless memory maintenance and source checking addressed it.”

   **Fix:** Explain that stale statements require checking the source content, while an unavailable projection motivates fallback. Keep K2’s claim limited to agreement with the files.

6. **Location: `CACHES.md:518`, Example numbers by case. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “On **tokens read**, full-text wins much earlier: on SEZA-sized memory, a single broad term already floods grep’s output. The B1.1 comparison measures exactly that.”

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** Large grep output establishes a reason to compare routes, not a full-text win. The SEZA measurement did not measure the paired reading path and found full-text misses. B1.1 also requires no more misses.

   **Source lines — quoted:** SZ, `runs/20261012-caches-numbers/nodes/seza.md:337`: “n=5, no verdict.” Its `:349` says it recorded “bytes printed and rank, not the full reading path of an agent”.

### Verdict
REPAIR — The changes since review are conceptually sound, but the text contains a direct requirement contradiction on fallback behaviour, leftover references counting three caches/stores instead of two, an inaccurate provenance description of moved text, a mis-cited absence finding, and an unaligned rerun trigger in K3.2.

### Findings

1. **Fallback on missing embedding model contradicts Core Requirement Rd3.3**
   - **Location**: `CACHES.md:148-149`
   - **Quoted text**: `"If the cache file or SQLite's FTS5 is missing, the tool answers with grep and says so in every answer (Rd3.2) (S-F9); the same for a missing embedding model (my reading, extending S-F9)."`
   - **Why it misleads or is wrong**: Stating that a missing embedding model does "the same" specifies that it falls back directly to grep. This directly contradicts requirement Rd3.3, which requires a missing embedding model to fall back to full-text search first if available, and only to grep if full-text is absent.
   - **Source line that shows it (quoted)**: `CACHES.md:442-443`: `"- **Rd3.3** A missing embedding model falls back to full-text if it is there, and to grep if not; the answer says so. *(my reading, extending S-F9; *proposed*: full-text before grep.)* (was R4.2)"`
   - **Severity**: High
   - **Fix**: Update line 148–149 to state that a missing embedding model falls back to full-text search first if present, and to grep if not, citing Rd3.3.

2. **Leftover text counts three caches and three stores for memory entries**
   - **Location**: `CACHES.md:54` and `CACHES.md:549`
   - **Quoted text**: Line 54: `"None of the three caches is needed today **for memory entries**."` and Line 549: `"K2 (stale never answers) matters more here than any of the three stores."`
   - **Why it misleads or is wrong**: The code graph was moved out to its own project (`docs/code-graph/CODE_GRAPH.md`). The document title (`# Memory Caches: SQLite Full-Text and Vector`), the vision statement, and line 298–299 establish that there are only two memory caches (full-text and vector). Counting "three caches" and "three stores" for memory entries are unedited leftovers from before the code graph was separated.
   - **Source line that shows it (quoted)**: `runs/20261013-caches-design-read/inputs/caches-vision.md:27`: `"The caches are two optional indexes over the project's markdown memory — full-text search and vector search."` and `CACHES.md:298-299`: `"They serve the vision... and cover the two memory caches, full-text and vector; the code graph is its own project (docs/code-graph/)."`
   - **Severity**: Medium
   - **Fix**: Change `"None of the three caches"` to `"Neither cache"` at line 54, and change `"any of the three stores"` to `"either store"` at line 549.

3. **Provenance note asserts that "Measured here" adds a routing rule for code and docs**
   - **Location**: `CACHES.md:44-46`

---
name: requirement-check
description: Evaluate a rule, requirement, threshold or algorithm against requirement-quality criteria before applying or writing it down, and ask the person which criteria it fails. Use when someone states a rule, requirement, constraint, threshold, algorithm or design guideline; when writing or editing a normative line in a document, plan or config; or when a rule reads as arbitrary, unattributed or impossible to check.
---

# Requirement check

A general standard (RFC 2119 / 8174), installed globally — not part of the Ancient Games framework. The
worked failure in `meaningful-names` cites the Ancient Games as one example.

A short gate between **hearing a rule** and **acting on it**. It exists because rules get written down that nobody
can trace, that count two different things at once, or that nothing can ever fail — and the cost lands months later,
when nobody can say whether the rule still means anything.

Run it when someone states a rule, and run it on yourself before writing a normative line into any document.

## The eight questions

Ask them of **one statement**. Say which it passes and which it fails, then **ask the person about the failures** —
do not fill the gaps yourself.

1. **Source.** Who said this, and where? Can you point to the exact line? *"Mine"* is a valid answer. Unlabelled is
   not.
2. **One thing.** An "and", an "or", a "/", or a second number means it is **two** statements. Split them, and give
   each its own source.
3. **Unit.** Every number says what it counts. Attempts or hours? Roles, or processes running at once? Bytes or
   files?
4. **Can it fail.** Name one concrete input that would make it fail. `TBD`, `—`, `0` and empty must be among the
   failing inputs. If you cannot name one, it is not checkable — it is a wish.
5. **Weak words.** *like, about, appropriate, as needed, logical, fast, enough, reasonable, only has to, TBD.* Replace
   each with a value, **or mark the value open**. Never invent the number.
6. **Level.** Is it **MUST** (a gate that stops work), **SHOULD** (a default that may be overridden with a stated
   reason), or **guidance**? A safety bound earns MUST. **A method does not** — see the RFC note below.
7. **Basis.** Measured, quoted from a source, or a guess? A guess is fine **if it is labelled** — `n=0`, with when it
   will be looked at again.
8. **Links.** Does it claim a relation to another rule — *"half of"*, *"pairs with"*, *"the same as"*, *"the runtime
   part"*? The person probably did not say that. **A link is its own claim and needs its own source.**

## Two rules for the agent

- **Never fill a gap with your own value.** A missing unit, threshold or test goes back as a question. Inventing it
  is exactly how a rule becomes unattributable later, and the invention is indistinguishable from the original by
  the time anyone notices.
- **Never add structure that was not given.** No halves, no numbered reasons, no ordered pairs, no named phases,
  unless the person said so. Quote them, state the operative sentence, stop. If a relationship between two rules is
  wanted, that is a separate claim with its own evidence.

## Two verified facts, worth quoting

- **RFC 2119 §6, "Guidance in the use of these Imperatives":** imperatives *"must be used with care and sparingly…
  they MUST only be used where it is actually required… they must not be used to try to impose a particular method."*
  Most design rules are method. Method does not earn MUST.
- **RFC 8174 (updates 2119):** the key words *"have the meanings specified herein only when they are in all
  capitals."* Lowercase *must* and *should* carry no defined requirement meaning. If nothing marks which lines are
  requirements, a reader cannot tell rules from explanation.

## How to answer

Evaluate first, in a table or a short list — **pass / FAIL per question**. Then ask the failing ones as questions,
one line each, with the options if you can see them. Two or three real questions beat eight formalities.

Do not report a rule as complete when you chose its missing value. Say which values you left open.

## A worked failure

> *"At most 5 subagent roles per run and at most 3 running at once."*

Four of the eight fail: **Source** — nobody could name one; **One thing** — a count of roles *and* a count of
concurrent processes; **Unit** — two different units, one word ("subagent") used for both; **Level** — a method
preference written as a cap. The sentence would not parse, and it took four days to find out why. The checklist
catches it in thirty seconds.

The same checklist, run on *"No single call runs longer than the ceiling: 8 hours"*, passes all eight — because it
names who set it, counts one thing, uses a unit, and some input (a 40-hour plan) makes it fail.

## Limits

The quality characteristics behind these questions (necessary, appropriate, unambiguous, complete, singular,
feasible, verifiable, correct, conforming) come from requirements-engineering sources — IEEE 830, ISO/IEC/IEEE 29148
and the INCOSE *Guide to Writing Requirements* — which were **not read** when this skill was written: they are
paywalled or blocked, and the list is recalled rather than verified. The two RFC points above **were** read, and the
quotes are checked. Treat the rest as a good working list, not as a citation.

Origin: `docs/research/20261005-requirement-statements/FINDINGS.md` in the Ancient Games project, 2026-10-05.

# Caches design — read review (round 1, opencode)

### Verdict

**REPAIR** — the change set is sound in substance and its citations hold almost everywhere, but seven lines still contradict the accepted requirements or the vision (a leftover third cache, a rule that drops EJ's decision, a `K3.2` rerun event that cannot fire, a B1.1 note with no home in Rd4, benchmark tables carrying none of the caveats the document itself lists as open, and one number attributed to a file that does not contain it).

### Findings

1. **`CACHES.md:54` — "None of the three caches is needed today **for memory entries**."** *Medium.* The code graph is out of scope; the accepted vision says two caches, and this document's own title says two. The same leftover count reappears at `:549` ("matters more here than any of the three stores"). *Shows:* `caches-vision.md:27` — "The caches are two optional indexes over the project's markdown memory — full-text search and vector search"; `CACHES.md:24` — "# Memory Caches: SQLite Full-Text and Vector". *Fix:* say "the two caches" at both places (or "the stores measured here" at `:549`, where three routes really were measured).

2. **`CACHES.md:413-414` (K3.2) — "The comparison is rerun when an agent notes a miss, and when EJ asks."** *Medium.* K3.1 covers both caches ("its B1 comparison"), but a miss is only definable once full-text exists, so for a full-text-only project this is the circular event the change to B1.1 just removed; a full-text cache could then only be rechecked when EJ asks. *Shows:* `CACHES.md:327-329` (B1.1's own event) — "It runs when EJ asks, or when an agent notes in its journal that `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read through many hits to reach an entry"; `CACHES.md:452-455` (Rd4.1) — "When an agent finds an entry that `INDEX.md`, grep and full-text all missed". *Fix:* give K3.2 both rerun events — B1.1's for the full-text cache, B1.2's for the vector.

3. **`CACHES.md:327-329` (B1.1) with `CACHES.md:451-455` (Rd4) — the trigger event has no home in Rd4.** *Medium.* B1.1 fires on "an agent notes in its journal that `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read through many hits to reach an entry", but Rd4.1 defines only one note type (misses) and then rules the rest out: "Every other question goes unrecorded: a log of every search costs every search, and the comparisons do not need it." An agent following Rd4 literally never makes the note, so the full-text trigger again has no firing event. *Fix:* add the cheap second note to Rd4.1 ("and, for the full-text trigger, a note when `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read through many hits"), or make B1.1's event EJ-asks-only.

4. **`CACHES.md:263-276` — the benchmark tables carry neither caveat the document lists as open.** *Medium.* BM-9 states the bound flatly — "Every route answers in at most 15 ms per question; the slowest was grep on E04, at 14.7 ms" — while `Unresolved after review` rows 1 and 2 record two unclosed problems about these very numbers, and "Who wrote each check" names the audit without what it found. *Shows:* `nodes/audit-0.json` — "rg's measured seconds include a `git ls-files` subprocess on every query, about 1.2 ms of rg's ~7.4 ms mean … An agent running rg would not pay this."; `nodes/build.opencode.stdout.md:68` — "`newest_source_mtime` moved mid-run (4016 → 4017 chunks) … it means these numbers are a snapshot of a corpus in motion. Re-run before the tables are used." *Fix:* one line in "Who wrote each check" carrying both (rg's timing includes a subprocess an agent would not pay; the corpus moved mid-run, so the tables await a rerun on a stable snapshot).

5. **`CACHES.md:103-104` — "an `INDEX.md` scan, under 0.01 s and minimal tokens (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`)."** *Medium.* `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` contains no timing and no token figure at all (its only cost statement is that `INDEX.md` "must fit the strictest start-file limit"); the number is one engine's reading, recorded as a disagreement. This is the same defect class the blind review failed the document on (`review.claude.md`: "No invented values: FAIL. One unsourced number"). *Shows:* `runs/20261012-caches-enhance/DECISION-RULES.md` D4 — "opencode: **<0.01 s** scan, minimal header-only tokens (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`); codex: **time GAP and tokens GAP**". *Fix:* attribute it to D4 in the run record and mark it as opencode's reading, or drop the number.

6. **`CACHES.md:152-153` — "**Index only active entries** … the same set as `INDEX.md`, with `covers` stored beside each row."** *Medium.* B4 now makes the entry headers the reference and `INDEX.md` the generated copy, but the bullet B4 cites as its source still names `INDEX.md` as the set. A reader who follows B4's citation lands on the rule the last review had changed. *Shows:* `CACHES.md:373-375` (B4.1) — "The headers are the reference, not `INDEX.md`, which is generated from them (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` K6.1)"; `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md:276` — "**K6.1** `INDEX.md` is generated from `entries/`". *Fix:* "the same set as the headers say, which is what `INDEX.md` is generated from".

7. **`CACHES.md:44-47` — the header note describes text that has moved.** *Low.* "the section **Measured here: development and testing** adds measurements on this repo and a routing rule for code and docs" — the code-and-docs routing rule left with Part 2; and "Its conflict with the vision for code and docs is left to EJ" contradicts the answer-first's settled statement. *Shows:* `CACHES.md:60-61` — "it is outside the vision, and its proposals to widen the vision were not adopted"; `CACHES.md:132-136` — "### Part 2 — code and docs / Moved to the code-graph project". *Fix:* say the routing rule moved with Part 2, and that the vision was kept for memory entries only.

8. **`CACHES.md:446` (Rd3.4) — "(G-F5, failure mode 5)".** *Low.* The provenance note says the node uses `F5` twice, so a bare `G-F5` is ambiguous, and failure mode 5 belongs to the drift study (the "Rules" bullet gets this right with `G-F5 drift`). *Shows:* `CACHES.md:28-29` — "`G-F5 drift` means the drift study and `G-F5 Serena` means Serena"; `code-graph.opencode.stdout.md:218-220` — "**Failure mode 5 — a mistyped cache path is indistinguishable from staleness.**". *Fix:* cite `G-F5 drift`.

9. **`CACHES.md:143` and `:366` — `G-F5 drift` cited without the label the vision now carries.** *Low.* A reader of this document alone cannot tell the drift measurement is a study of a code-graph tool. *Shows:* `caches-vision.md:46-47` — "`G-F5`: a study of a code-graph tool, kept as evidence of how a patched index fails". *Fix:* add the same parenthetical once, in `Rules for every cache`.

10. **`CACHES.md:233` — "results in `bench/results.md`, ids `BM-*` below", and `:316` "Measured as the code-and-docs benchmark was (BM-1 to BM-9)".** *Low.* `results.md` carries no `BM-*` ids (its headings are Category A–E, Build, Determinism check), so every `BM-*` citation — including the two B1.1/B1.2 ones that adopt "the benchmark's measure" — is unresolvable at the named source without inferring the mapping. *Fix:* say the ids are this document's labels for `results.md`'s categories A–E, or name the category next to the id once.

11. **`CACHES.md:438` (Rd3.1, "(M6)") and `:97` ("M17, M18, M19") — CONTESTED ids cited bare.** *Low.* B2 discloses M6's status; the other two places do not, and M17 is CONTESTED in its source too. *Shows:* `RECOMMENDATION.md:35` — "M6 … CONTESTED (opencode: use `state.sqlite` as the structured state store…)"; `RECOMMENDATION.md:46,55-58` — "M17 correction (repair 2). M17 is **CONTESTED**, not MAJORITY". *Fix:* disclose once at the top of Part 1's evidence notes, or mark each citation.

12. **`CACHES.md:117` — "For memory entries all six stay open."** *Low.* D1 is the store-and-order question the document now answers in structure (full-text conditional on B1.1; vector only after it, *proposed*), so "all six" overstates what is open. *Shows:* `DECISION-RULES.md:136-140` (D1 — "opencode: **FTS5 first** … codex: **baseline first** … claude: **no store at all**"); `CACHES.md:212-216` — "So the vector index comes after the full-text index, never instead of it. This is a **proposed** policy". *Fix:* say which part of D1 the requirements now decide and which stays open (whether a store is needed at all).

13. **`CACHES.md:98` — "A map of the whole repo | none | `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md`, `INDEX.md` | M18, G-F18".** *Low.* `G-F18` is the in-repo tool's determinism and stdout-only output; it says nothing about a repo map, and the run record this row was merged from cites other ids for it. *Shows:* `DECISION-RULES.md:61` — "| Give me a map of the whole repo | **none** — `M18` forbids loading a whole-graph dump … | `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md` and `INDEX.md` | … | M18, G-F12, G-F7b, s3-F24 |"; `code-graph.opencode.stdout.md:181` (F18). *Fix:* cite M18 with the run record's other ids.

### Unresolved table

- **BM-9 speed bound and the `git ls-files` overhead** — still open, and it bears on the text: nothing in "Measured here" carries the disclosure two reviewers ask for, and BM-9 states the bound flatly (finding 4). codex's narrower claim is checkable and consistent with the audit: the overhead biases grep upward, so removing it cannot break the 15 ms bound.
- **Corpus snapshot stability during the benchmark build** — half settled, half open. Settled: the current text no longer claims no rerun happened ("Rebuilding from scratch gave the same answers on all 180 question-route pairs"), so agy's and codex's "do not say no rerun happened" is moot. Open: the builder's "Re-run before the tables are used" is carried nowhere, and every table is used in the text (finding 4).
- **The audit's lower-severity findings** — still open as a set, with no disposition recorded anywhere. codex's two factual points check out against `audit-0.json`: one finding is literally "No problem found; recorded so it is not re-checked", and the vector-deduplication finding is there. opencode's "some of them bear on the text" holds: that finding (vec fetches 10 chunks then drops duplicate paths, so it often returns fewer than 10) explains the very "top 5" measure B1.1 and B1.2 adopt.
- **Discovery versus exhaustive routes in the memory "none" rules** — still open. Part 1 keeps its `none` rows unchanged and nothing in the design settles it; two of three reviewers now hold codex's position, so one sentence from EJ would close it.

### What holds

- The answer-first's substance: nothing is needed today, `INDEX.md`/grep/git answer the questions, each cache has an observable trigger, `.cache/` is optional, gitignored and rebuilt (`M4`, `M6` cited correctly), and the SEZA/staleness argument is right.
- Part 1's rule and table after the change: the `INDEX.md` size trigger is gone everywhere, the code-graph row points at `docs/code-graph/`, and every `S-F`/`V-F`/`M*`/`s3-F*` id I checked resolves to what the row claims (`V-F1`, `V-F7`, `V-F20`, `V-F21`, `V-F22`, `V-F23`, `M13`, `M18`, `M19`, `s3-F29`, `s3-F34` all confirmed in their nodes).
- "Rules for every cache": the heading and B4/K1/K2's source labels were updated together, the `status` drop is consistent (`S-F2`'s UNINDEXED columns → `kind` and `covers` only), and the four rules match K2/B3/Rd3 (finding 6 aside).
- SQLite full-text: five whys and 5W1H, with every number verified against `sqlite-fts.claude.md` (0.038 s / 0.060 s at 10,000; 0.53 s rebuild; 0.104 s header script; 0.148 s at 50,000 with the one-word timing discarded; 1,144 docs / 1.7M tokens / 5.8K vs 226K; ~37,000 entries; `unicode61` and the quoted `D-0012`; SQLite 3.45.1 verified 2026-10-12), and the trigger is the B1.1 comparison.
- Vector: five whys and 5W1H verified against `vector.claude.md` (F3/F4/F5/F6/F7/F10/F11/F12/F13/F18/F19/F20/F21/F24, including the `<200,000 tokens` bound and the 400–650 entry arithmetic), RRF attributed to the vector node §Q5, no invented numbers.
- "Measured here": the per-category table matches `results.md` line for line; BM-6's means and totals recompute correctly; BM-7 recomputes from `nodes/runtime-recall.json` (graph 0.78; rg/fts/hybrid 0.59; vec 0.04) and BM-8's per-question claims hold; BM-9's rebuild sizes match the three `.cache/*.sqlite` files on disk; the 529 tests / 31–33 s figure is in `state.md:51`; the graph's "over 2 questions" disclosure is there; the proxy's four weaknesses and the "repair not re-audited" disclosure are honest.

### Verdict

### Findings

1. **Location: K2.3, `CACHES.md:402`. Severity: high.**

   **Quoted text:** “The full-text and vector indexes are rebuilt by one command, in one step. If either fails, neither replaces the old ones”.

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** This unconditionally requires both indexes even when only full-text has been approved. It can make a full-text rebuild depend on a vector index or embedding model that must not yet exist.

   **Source line — quoted:** B1.2, `CACHES.md:344`: “it is built only after B1.1 has fired”; B5.1, `CACHES.md:381`: “the full-text path still works.”

   **Fix:** Apply the joint rebuild and joint publication rule when both indexes are built. State that a full-text-only installation rebuilds full-text alone.

2. **Location: Rd4.1, `CACHES.md:454`. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “Every other question goes unrecorded”.

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** B1.1 also uses a journal note about a successful search that required reading many hits. That question is not necessarily a miss, so the blanket prohibition conflicts with the new full-text comparison event.

   **Source line — quoted:** B1.1, `CACHES.md:327`: “when an agent notes in its journal that `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read through many hits to reach an entry”.

   **Fix:** Say ordinary searches need no log, while preserving the specific B1.1 reading-friction notes alongside Rd4.1 miss notes.

3. **Location: `CACHES.md:149`, Rules for every cache. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “the same for a missing embedding model”.

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** The preceding sentence sends the query to grep. The accepted Rd3.3 now sends it to available full-text first.

   **Source line — quoted:** Rd3.3, `CACHES.md:442`: “A missing embedding model falls back to full-text if it is there, and to grep if not; the answer says so.”

   **Fix:** Copy that fallback order into the summary rule, including disclosure of the route used.

4. **Location: K3.1 and Rd4.1, `CACHES.md:411`. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “for the vector index, on the noted misses”.

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** Journal notes persist, but their target entries can subsequently become superseded or retired. The design does not say how those historical targets participate in a comparison against today’s active-only index. A formerly valid target can become deliberately unsearchable, then appear to demonstrate that the cache stopped paying.

   **Source line — quoted:** B4.1, `CACHES.md:371`: “every file in `entries/` whose own header says `status: active`, and nothing else”. Rd4.2, `CACHES.md:457`: questions are written “from the entries that exist then”.

   **Fix:** State how journal misses are reconciled with the comparison’s current active corpus. Preserve the historical notes, but distinguish an ineligible old target from a retrieval failure.

5. **Location: `CACHES.md:549`, Example numbers by case. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “K2 (stale never answers) matters more here than any of the three stores.”

   **Why it misleads — my reading:** This connects stale memory content to cache freshness. K2 detects disagreement between an index and its entry files; it cannot detect an outdated statement faithfully indexed from an unchanged file. The missing projection is instead relevant to Rd3.

   **Source lines — quoted:** K2.1, `CACHES.md:395`: “compares the recorded content hash with the entry files”. CX, `runs/20261012-caches-numbers/nodes/codex.md:341`: outdated information remains “unless memory maintenance and source checking addressed it.”

   **Fix:** Explain that stale statements require checking the source content, while an unavailable projection motivates fallback. Keep K2’s claim limited to agreement with the files.

6. **Location: `CACHES.md:518`, Example numbers by case. Severity: medium.**

   **Quoted text:** “On **tokens read**, full-text wins much earlier: on SEZA-sized memory, a single broad term already floods grep’s output. The B1.1 comparison measures exactly that.”

   **Source lines — quoted:** SZ, `runs/20261012-caches-numbers/nodes/seza.md:337`: “n=5, no verdict.” Its `:349` says it recorded “bytes printed and rank, not the full reading path of an agent”.

# Caches design — read review (round 1, opencode)

### Verdict

**REPAIR** — the change set is sound in substance and its citations hold almost everywhere, but seven lines still contradict the accepted requirements or the vision (a leftover third cache, a rule that drops EJ's decision, a `K3.2` rerun event that cannot fire, a B1.1 note with no home in Rd4, benchmark tables carrying none of the caveats the document itself lists as open, and one number attributed to a file that does not contain it).

### Findings

1. **`CACHES.md:54` — "None of the three caches is needed today **for memory entries**."** *Medium.* The code graph is out of scope; the accepted vision says two caches, and this document's own title says two. The same leftover count reappears at `:549` ("matters more here than any of the three stores"). *Shows:* `caches-vision.md:27` — "The caches are two optional indexes over the project's markdown memory — full-text search and vector search"; `CACHES.md:24` — "# Memory Caches: SQLite Full-Text and Vector". *Fix:* say "the two caches" at both places (or "the stores measured here" at `:549`, where three routes really were measured).

2. **`CACHES.md:413-414` (K3.2) — "The comparison is rerun when an agent notes a miss, and when EJ asks."** *Medium.* K3.1 covers both caches ("its B1 comparison"), but a miss is only definable once full-text exists, so for a full-text-only project this is the circular event the change to B1.1 just removed; a full-text cache could then only be rechecked when EJ asks. *Shows:* `CACHES.md:327-329` (B1.1's own event) — "It runs when EJ asks, or when an agent notes in its journal that `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read through many hits to reach an entry"; `CACHES.md:452-455` (Rd4.1) — "When an agent finds an entry that `INDEX.md`, grep and full-text all missed". *Fix:* give K3.2 both rerun events — B1.1's for the full-text cache, B1.2's for the vector.

3. **`CACHES.md:327-329` (B1.1) with `CACHES.md:451-455` (Rd4) — the trigger event has no home in Rd4.** *Medium.* B1.1 fires on "an agent notes in its journal that `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read through many hits to reach an entry", but Rd4.1 defines only one note type (misses) and then rules the rest out: "Every other question goes unrecorded: a log of every search costs every search, and the comparisons do not need it." An agent following Rd4 literally never makes the note, so the full-text trigger again has no firing event. *Fix:* add the cheap second note to Rd4.1 ("and, for the full-text trigger, a note when `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read through many hits"), or make B1.1's event EJ-asks-only.

4. **`CACHES.md:263-276` — the benchmark tables carry neither caveat the document lists as open.** *Medium.* BM-9 states the bound flatly — "Every route answers in at most 15 ms per question; the slowest was grep on E04, at 14.7 ms" — while `Unresolved after review` rows 1 and 2 record two unclosed problems about these very numbers, and "Who wrote each check" names the audit without what it found. *Shows:* `nodes/audit-0.json` — "rg's measured seconds include a `git ls-files` subprocess on every query, about 1.2 ms of rg's ~7.4 ms mean … An agent running rg would not pay this."; `nodes/build.opencode.stdout.md:68` — "`newest_source_mtime` moved mid-run (4016 → 4017 chunks) … it means these numbers are a snapshot of a corpus in motion. Re-run before the tables are used." *Fix:* one line in "Who wrote each check" carrying both (rg's timing includes a subprocess an agent would not pay; the corpus moved mid-run, so the tables await a rerun on a stable snapshot).

5. **`CACHES.md:103-104` — "an `INDEX.md` scan, under 0.01 s and minimal tokens (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`)."** *Medium.* `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` contains no timing and no token figure at all (its only cost statement is that `INDEX.md` "must fit the strictest start-file limit"); the number is one engine's reading, recorded as a disagreement. This is the same defect class the blind review failed the document on (`review.claude.md`: "No invented values: FAIL. One unsourced number"). *Shows:* `runs/20261012-caches-enhance/DECISION-RULES.md` D4 — "opencode: **<0.01 s** scan, minimal header-only tokens (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`); codex: **time GAP and tokens GAP**". *Fix:* attribute it to D4 in the run record and mark it as opencode's reading, or drop the number.

6. **`CACHES.md:152-153` — "**Index only active entries** … the same set as `INDEX.md`, with `covers` stored beside each row."** *Medium.* B4 now makes the entry headers the reference and `INDEX.md` the generated copy, but the bullet B4 cites as its source still names `INDEX.md` as the set. A reader who follows B4's citation lands on the rule the last review had changed. *Shows:* `CACHES.md:373-375` (B4.1) — "The headers are the reference, not `INDEX.md`, which is generated from them (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` K6.1)"; `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md:276` — "**K6.1** `INDEX.md` is generated from `entries/`". *Fix:* "the same set as the headers say, which is what `INDEX.md` is generated from".

7. **`CACHES.md:44-47` — the header note describes text that has moved.** *Low.* "the section **Measured here: development and testing** adds measurements on this repo and a routing rule for code and docs" — the code-and-docs routing rule left with Part 2; and "Its conflict with the vision for code and docs is left to EJ" contradicts the answer-first's settled statement. *Shows:* `CACHES.md:60-61` — "it is outside the vision, and its proposals to widen the vision were not adopted"; `CACHES.md:132-136` — "### Part 2 — code and docs / Moved to the code-graph project". *Fix:* say the routing rule moved with Part 2, and that the vision was kept for memory entries only.

10. **`CACHES.md:233` — "results in `bench/results.md`, ids `BM-*` below", and `:316` "Measured as the code-and-docs benchmark was (BM-1 to BM-9)".** *Low.* `results.md` carries no `BM-*` ids (its headings are Category A–E, Build, Determinism check), so every `BM-*` citation — including the two B1.1/B1.2 ones that adopt "the benchmark's measure" — is unresolvable at the named source without inferring the mapping. *Fix:* say the ids are this document's labels for `results.md`'s categories A–E, or name the category next to the id once.

11. **`CACHES.md:438` (Rd3.1, "(M6)") and `:97` ("M17, M18, M19") — CONTESTED ids cited bare.** *Low.* B2 discloses M6's status; the other two places do not, and M17 is CONTESTED in its source too. *Shows:* `RECOMMENDATION.md:35` — "M6 … CONTESTED (opencode: use `state.sqlite` as the structured state store…)"; `RECOMMENDATION.md:46,55-58` — "M17 correction (repair 2). 12. **`CACHES.md:117` — "For memory entries all six stay open."** *Low.* D1 is the store-and-order question the document now answers in structure (full-text conditional on B1.1; vector only after it, *proposed*), so "all six" overstates what is open. *Shows:* `DECISION-RULES.md:136-140` (D1 — "opencode: **FTS5 first** … codex: **baseline first** … claude: **no store at all**"); `CACHES.md:212-216` — "So the vector index comes after the full-text index, never instead of it.
