    51	
    52	## The answer first
    53	
    54	None of the three caches is needed today **for memory entries**. `project-memory/` does not exist yet, so nothing has
    55	been measured on it. My reading of the research is that at the sizes measured elsewhere, `INDEX.md`, grep and git
    56	answer the questions each cache would answer. Each cache below has a
    57	**trigger**: a condition that can be observed and that makes it worth building. None of them is built before its
    58	trigger fires.
    59	
    60	The caches are for **memory entries** only: that is the vision EJ confirmed on 2026-10-12. The measured section below
    61	on code and docs is kept as evidence; it is outside the vision, and its proposals to widen the vision were not adopted.
    62	
    63	This follows the design's own rule: `.cache/` is optional, gitignored and rebuilt from the files, and its absence
    64	must not stop anyone reading memory (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`; recommendation M4, M6). It also follows the repo's own
    65	history of building graphs nobody queried (`docs/KNOWLEDGE_GRAPH_DESIGN.md` §1, quoted in `G-F21`).
    66	
    67	## When to use which store
    68	
    69	**Part 1 is for memory entries** (`project-memory/`), reasoned from the research and the run record. Part 2, for code
    70	and docs, has moved to the code-graph project. A cache is derived and optional and is never the source of truth; once
    71	its trigger fires it is a cheaper way to reach the same entry.
    72	
    73	### Part 1 — memory entries (corpus: `project-memory/`)
    74	
    75	At query time the memory is answered by the files — `INDEX.md`, grep, git, or reading the entry — and the files are
    76	the source of truth, not the cache. The table below merges three engines asked independently, one store
    77	each: `claude` on the code graph, `codex` on the vector index, `opencode` on SQLite FTS5. The full 21-row table and
    78	the 30-row cost table, with every disagreement kept, are in `runs/20261012-caches-enhance/DECISION-RULES.md` — the
    79	run record. **This file is the live copy.**
    80	
    81	**The rule.** **SQLite FTS5** is for a free-text question whose words are likely in the entry text, once its
    82	comparison (B1.1) shows ranked results with snippets reach the right entry after reading less than `INDEX.md` plus grep
    83	(`S-F1`, `S-F4`). **The vector index** is for the same discovery when the question's wording differs from the
    84	entry's, once the documented miss trigger has fired (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). Everything else is the files.
    85	
    86	| the question | store | if not, this | evidence |
    87	|---|---|---|---|
    88	| What do we already know about X? (free text, words likely in the entry) | **FTS5** — conditional on its trigger (B1.1) | `INDEX.md`, grep, then read | S-F1, S-F4 |
    89	| Which prior entry matches when my words differ from its? (find by meaning) | **vector** — conditional on the miss trigger | `INDEX.md`, grep, then read | V-F11, V-F13, M4 |
    90	| A question about the code itself: what changing F reaches, which tests reach F | the code-graph project (`docs/code-graph/`) | — | — |
    91	| An exact identifier, an error string, a known `file:line` | none | literal grep or filename lookup, then read | V-F7, V-F21, s3-F29 |
    92	| Every active entry covering path X — completeness | none — a ranked top-k cannot prove completeness | exhaustive scan of `INDEX.md` and entry headers | V-F1, S-F13, G-F14 |
    93	| Proving absence ("have we never recorded…") | none — no hit is not proof | exhaustive search, then read | V-F20 |
    94	| A structured header question (status, `supersedes`, `depends_on`) | none | `INDEX.md` scan or a script over frontmatter; follow links by grep | V-F22, M13 |
    95	| Why did we choose this, or why is this line like this? | none | the entry's **Why:** and its evidence; `git blame`, `git log -S` | M19, SOURCE_OF_TRUTH |
    96	| Is this decision still true of the code? | none | read the entry and the code before acting on it; a mismatch is reported to EJ | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH Rd5 |
    97	| What should I load when resuming? | none | `INDEX.md` and the task's `state.md` | M17, M18, M19 |
    98	| A map of the whole repo | none | `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md`, `INDEX.md` | M18, G-F18 |
    99	
   100	**Costs, and where there are none.** Measured on other corpora or on this machine, never on a real
   101	`project-memory/`: grep over 10,000 synthetic entries, 0.038 s for one word and 0.060 s for a structured chain
   102	(`S-F13`); grep plus opening every hit, about 226K tokens per question (`S-F4`, `S-F13` — opencode's figure, and D3
   103	records that the other two report no such measurement; `S-F4` measures other corpora, not memory entries); an
   104	`INDEX.md` scan, under 0.01 s and minimal tokens
   105	(`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`); reading one entry, roughly 270 tokens by the 4-characters-per-token assumption (`S-F5`, not
   106	measured); a git last-change lookup at the tool's timing resolution (`G-F12`). **`GAP` for everything that matters
   107	most**: no FTS5-vs-grep measurement on short entries (`S-F5`), no vector measurement on a corpus shaped like
   108	`entries/` (`V-F11`), and no real `project-memory/` corpus. This repo's code and docs are measured in **Measured
   109	here: development and testing** below.
   110	
   111	**Still unresolved.** Six disagreements the three engines did not settle, kept as positions and not averaged (D1–D6
   112	in the run record): whether FTS5 or the vector index comes first, or whether memory needs a store at all; whether the
   113	FTS/vector benefit has been measured at all; the true token cost of grep-plus-open; the cost of `INDEX.md`; whether
   114	`INDEX.md` carries `covers:` and status; and what counts as a *fired* trigger. **For code and docs, the measurements
   115	settle three of them** (my reading): D1, full-text before vector (BM-4, BM-5); D2, the full-text benefit is measured
   116	on docs (BM-4, BM-6); D3, grep printed about 4,470 characters to the right file on decision questions, not ~226K
   117	tokens (BM-4). For memory entries all six stay open.
   118	
   119	**Why no SQLite is visible in this repo.** Three reasons, and none is a rejection. (Since 2026-10-12 three SQLite
   120	caches do exist, in the measurement run's workspace: `runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/.cache/` holds `fts.sqlite`,
   121	`vec.sqlite` and `graph.sqlite`, 9.5, 10.2 and 4.9 MB (BM-9). They are gitignored and rebuilt there, not part of the
   122	product.) The patterns are gitignored and
   123	the indexes are rebuilt on demand. The SQLite this repo does have, `ancient_games/index.py`,
   124	indexes the **referee's journal**, not memory: it is the working precedent for this exact pattern (7 tables, derived,
   125	~8 ms per journal) but it answers a different question. And the memory cache is gated behind a trigger that has not
   126	fired, because `project-memory/` does not exist yet.
   127	
   128	A fourth reason belongs to the first draft. Its research asked only whether each cache is needed to search memory
   129	*entries*, so it measured nothing on the questions asked during development and testing. The section **Measured here:
   130	development and testing** measures those.
   131	
   132	### Part 2 — code and docs
   133	
   134	Moved to the code-graph project, `docs/code-graph/CODE_GRAPH.md` (EJ, 2026-10-12: *"others can accept and apply"*). It
   135	routed questions about this repo's code and docs, outside the caches vision; its evidence stays below in **Measured
   136	here: development and testing**.
   137	
   138	## Rules for every cache
   139	
   140	These apply to whichever cache is built. They come from how the researched tools fail.
   141	
   142	- **Full rebuild, never patched.** A live, incrementally updated index drifted silently from a clean rebuild: 4.3% of
   143	  edges wrong, with every integrity check passing (`G-F5 drift`). Its makers decided drift cannot be measured cheaply
   144	  (`G-F5c`). An FTS rebuild of 10,000 synthetic entries takes 0.53 s (`S-F13`).
   145	- **Rebuild before answering when stale.** Before each query, compare the recorded content hash of the
   146	  sources with the index and rebuild if they differ. Build into a temporary file and rename it over the old one
   147	  (`S-F9`, quoted: *"A stale index is worse than no index."*).
   148	- **Fall back when absent.** If the cache file or SQLite's FTS5 is missing, the tool answers with grep and says so in
   149	  every answer (Rd3.2) (`S-F9`); the same for a missing embedding model (my reading, extending `S-F9`). A mistyped
   150	  database path silently creates an empty database, which looks exactly like a stale one (`G-F5 drift`, failure
   151	  mode 5), so the tool opens the cache read-only and treats "no such file" as absent.
   152	- **Index only active entries** — for `project-memory/` — the same set as `INDEX.md`, with `covers` stored
   153	  beside each row (`V-F22`, my reading). Otherwise superseded entries come back looking current.
   154	
   155	## SQLite full-text index
   156	
   157	### Five whys
   158	
   159	1. **Why a full-text index?** To find entries by their words, ranked, with a snippet, instead of grep's unranked list
   160	   (`S-F1`, quoted: *"grep returns unranked matches."*).
   161	2. **Why does ranking matter?** Every hit the agent opens costs context. In the one controlled study, BM25 used 5.8K
   162	   tokens per question against 226K for an agent using grep over files, on its smallest corpus: 1,144 documents,
   163	   1.7M tokens (`S-F4`).
   164	3. **Why not build it now?** That study puts the point where BM25 overtakes the grep agent at around 10 million tokens
   165	   of text, and the grep agent led at the smallest sizes (`S-F4`). At our entry size that is about 37,000 entries
   166	   (`S-F5`, my reading via its 4-characters-per-token assumption). `INDEX.md` already lists every active entry. In the
   167	   researcher's own test, at 10,000 synthetic entries a one-word grep took 0.038 s and a grep chain for a header
   168	   question 0.06 s; at 50,000 the header chain took 0.15 s, and the one-word timing was invalid (`S-F13`). No study
   169	   covers a corpus of a few hundred to a few thousand short entries (`S-F5`: not measured).
   170	4. **Why might it be needed later?** If, on a set of the project's own questions, `INDEX.md` plus grep makes an agent
   171	   read more to reach the right entry than ranked search would. The B1.1 comparison measures exactly that (EJ,
   172	   2026-10-12: *"the comparison for full-text"*).
   173	5. **Why only as a cache?** The markdown is the source of truth, and the cache must be rebuildable and skippable (M6).
   174	
   175	Structured questions such as "every active decision covering path X" or "what supersedes Y" do not need SQLite. A
   176	short script reading the entry headers answers them in 0.1 s at 10,000 entries (`S-F13`). That measures time, not
   177	tokens; my reading is that the script and an SQL query return the same list of entries, so the agent reads the same
   178	amount either way (not measured).
   179	
   180	### 5W1H
   181	
   182	| | |
   183	|---|---|
   184	| **Who** | Built and rebuilt by a script with no model call. Read by any agent on demand. EJ decides to build it when the trigger fires. |
   185	| **What** | One FTS5 table with `kind` and `covers` as `UNINDEXED` columns (`S-F2`). One row per active entry file: the research leaves the row grain open (`S-F17`); my reading is that an entry of one or two sentences is already one section. The default `unicode61` tokenizer: in the researcher's own one-row test, a plausible change to keep paths whole made ordinary queries return zero rows silently, and ids such as `D-0012` had to be quoted (`S-F16`). |
   186	| **When** | Built when its B1.1 comparison passes and EJ decides (B1.0). Rebuilt before any query that finds it stale. Never read at session start. |
   187	| **Where** | `project-memory/.cache/index.sqlite`, gitignored. |
   188	| **Why** | The five whys above. |
   189	| **How** | One stdlib `sqlite3` script, the smallest option in the SQLite node (§5, option B): rebuild to a temporary file and rename; compare the recorded content hash for staleness, so a deleted or uncommitted change is caught (K1.1, K2.1); fall back to `rg`. Nothing to install: SQLite 3.45.1 with FTS5 works here (verified 2026-10-12). |
   190	
   191	## Vector index
   192	
   193	### Five whys
   194	
   195	1. **Why a vector index?** To find an entry by meaning when the question shares no words with it.
   196	2. **Why can't grep or full-text search do that?** They match words. But the evidence does not show vectors winning on
   197	   memory text. (They do win on some code tasks, `V-F5`, and on half the file-based pairs in `V-F3`.) On conversational
   198	   memory, grep returned inline beat vector search for every harness and model pair (`V-F3`). On a 500,000-document
   199	   corpus, BM25 beat vectors even on the questions built to favour them (`V-F4`). On decision-style records, a
   200	   structured graph and a vector memory tool were "largely equivalent" on relevance, and plain notes search did no
   201	   better on listing or supersession questions (s3-F34; `V-F1`).
   202	3. **Why not build it now?** No measurement exists on a corpus shaped like `entries/` (`V-F11`: not measured).
   203	   Anthropic's 2024 guidance is that under about 200,000 tokens a knowledge base can simply be put in the prompt
   204	   (`V-F8`), roughly 400–650 entries (`V-F12`, my reading). This design does not do that (it never loads the whole
   205	   tree), so this is only a bound: below it, retrieval quality is not what limits an agent (my reading). Vectors also
   206	   add failures of their own: negation (`V-F20`), exact ids and paths (`V-F21`), and a second rebuild step that can lag
   207	   the first (`V-F24`).
   208	4. **Why might it be needed later?** If agents keep missing entries that exist because their words differ from the
   209	   entry's. The trigger is a record of such misses: the entry existed, `INDEX.md`, grep and full-text search all missed
   210	   it, and it was found another way (`V-F13`, my reading). The vector index is worth building when, on those recorded
   211	   misses, the hybrid index finds entries full-text did not, and it does no worse on the other questions (B1.2).
   212	5. **Why hybrid only?** On memory-style text, the positive results found combine vectors with keyword search (`V-F6`,
   213	   `V-F7`; `V-F10` is unproven), and vectors alone were the weakest arm when results were returned inline (`V-F3`,
   214	   `V-F4`). So the vector index comes after the full-text index, never
   215	   instead of it. This is a **proposed** policy: on half the file-based pairs in `V-F3` vectors won alone (why 2
   216	   above), so the evidence does not compel it.
   217	
   218	### 5W1H
   219	
   220	| | |
   221	|---|---|
   222	| **Who** | Built and rebuilt by a script with no model call except the embedding model itself. Read by any agent on demand, through the same command as full-text search. EJ decides to build it when the trigger fires. |
   223	| **What** | One vector per active entry file, with the entry's `covers`, content hash and embedding-model id stored beside it (`V-F24`: a changed model makes every vector useless). Results merged with the full-text results by reciprocal rank fusion (vector node §Q5, from qmd's README). |
   224	| **When** | After the full-text index exists, and when the B1.2 comparison favours hybrid. Rebuilt with the full-text index, in the same step, so the two cannot disagree (`V-F24`, my reading). |
   225	| **Where** | `project-memory/.cache/`, beside `index.sqlite`, gitignored. |
   226	| **Why** | The five whys above. |
   227	| **How** | The smallest options in `V-F18`/`V-F19`: a static Model2Vec model with numpy (option A, no GPU) or `bge-small-en-v1.5` through FastEmbed (option B). Both are small next to the 16 GB GPU here. Embedding speed on this GPU is **not measured** (`V-F18`). No embedding runtime is installed today; numpy is (`V-F19`). |
   228	
   229	## Measured here: development and testing
   230	
   231	EJ's aim is that development and testing get faster and cheaper in tokens. So this section measures all three stores
   232	against grep on **this repo**: 490 files (123 `.py`, 367 `.md`, 4.8 MB), with 40 questions an agent asks while
   233	developing and testing it. Run: `runs/20261012-caches-routing/` (results in `bench/results.md`, ids `BM-*` below).
   234	
   235	**Who wrote each check, and what it does not establish.**
   236	- The **questions and answers** were written by codex (`gpt-6.1-sol`), each checked by an `rg` command it ran. Five
   237	  Claude agents re-checked all 40: 37 were correct and 3 were incomplete. Those 3 were completed.
   238	- **"Which tests run this code"** is also scored against per-test runtime coverage (`pytest-cov`, no model). That
   239	  counts in-process Python only, so it misses tests that run the code as a separate process.
   240	- The **harness** was written by opencode. A Claude audit found 5 high-severity biases, and a Claude repair fixed
   241	  them; the repair was **not re-audited**. Rebuilding from scratch gave the same answers on all 180 question-route
   242	  pairs.
   243	- **Token cost** is measured as *characters printed up to the first line that names a right file*. That is a proxy,
   244	  not a token count, and a weak one:
   245	  - it stops at the right *file*, not the right answer;
   246	  - misses are left out, so each route's mean is over a different set of questions;
   247	  - each route prints a different format.
   248	
   249	  No agent was run end to end.
   250	- One repo, one question set, **n = 6 to 10 per category**. None of this is a project-memory corpus.
   251	
   252	**What each route returns, per category** (symbol hit in the top 5 / characters read to the first line that names a
   253	right file):
   254	
   255	| | grep (`rg`) | SQLite full-text | vector | hybrid | code graph |
   256	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
   257	| **BM-1** exact identifier (8) | 0.75 / 920 | 0.63 / 356 | 0.0 / 837 | 0.5 / 91 | **0.75 / 43** |
   258	| **BM-2** concept in other words (8) | 0 / — | 0 / — | 0 (file 0.25) / 955 | 0 (file 0.13) / 1,427 | n/a |
   259	| **BM-3** structure and test impact (10) | 0.8 / 1,015 | **0.9 / 175** | 0.1 / 1,421 | 0.9 / 362 | 0.7 / **61** |
   260	| **BM-4** decision or reason in the docs (8) | 0.63 / 4,470 | **0.63 / 525** | 0.5 / 810 | 0.63 / 516 | 0.0 (over 2 questions) |
   261	| **BM-5** how to run or configure (6) | 0.17 / 6,875 | 0.33 / 1,146 | 0.5 / 569 | 0.5 / 1,120 | n/a |
   262	
   263	- **BM-6, all categories.** Characters printed up to the first right file (each mean over that route's hits only:
   264	  grep 26, full-text 25, vector 19, hybrid 26, graph 13): grep 2,688, full-text 451, hybrid 528, vector
   265	  923, graph 53 (graph over the 20 questions that name a symbol or file). Total output per answer: grep 4,304,
   266	  full-text 1,817.
   267	- **BM-7, test impact against runtime coverage** (C01–C04, n=4). The macro-average test-file recall of the tests that
   268	  really run the function each route found: graph 0.78; grep, full-text and hybrid 0.59; vector 0.04.
   269	- **BM-8, tests that run the code as a separate process** (C05–C07). The graph returns **nothing**, because no
   270	  call edge exists. Full-text search on the file name found 2 of 3; grep named a gold file on 2 of 3 and the
   271	  complete set on 1 of 3, printing up to 8.7K
   272	  characters.
   273	- **BM-9, speed.** Every route answers in at most 15 ms per question; the slowest was grep on E04, at 14.7 ms. Full
   274	  rebuilds take 0.17 s for full-text (9.5 MB), 0.94 s for vector (10.2 MB) and 0.89 s for the graph (4.9 MB); the
   275	  graph's own rebuild for `G-F12` was measured separately at 0.94 s. The full test suite, 529 tests, takes 31–33 s (3
   276	  runs).
   277	
   278	**What the numbers say (my reading).**
   279	1. **On this repo, speed is not the reason to use a cache.** Every route takes milliseconds (BM-9). The difference is
   280	   in **what the route prints**: full-text prints about a sixth of grep's characters before naming a right file
   281	   (BM-6). Whether that becomes a sixth of an agent's tokens is not measured. Where
   282	   the question names a symbol or file, the graph gets there in tens of characters (BM-1, BM-3); its BM-6 mean is over
   283	   those 20 questions only, so it is not a like-for-like ratio.
   284	2. **The vector index loses almost everywhere it was tried.** It never found an exact identifier (BM-1), and its
   285	   test-file recall against runtime coverage was 0.04 (BM-7). Its only edge is "how do I run or configure X" (BM-5,
   286	   n=6), which is
   287	   too small to act on. Nothing here contradicts the earlier rule that vectors come last and only as a hybrid.
   288	3. **No route answers a concept asked in other words in one shot** (BM-2). That still takes an agent searching and
   289	   reading. It is exactly the miss the vector trigger is meant to record.
   290	4. **No single store answers "which tests should I run".** The graph finds tests that reach the code indirectly
   291	   (BM-7), but is blind to tests that run it as a separate process (BM-8). Full-text search on the file name finds
   292	   the latter. An empty graph answer means *not seen*, never *no tests*. Even combined, the two routes
   293	   can miss affected tests: both missed C05 entirely, and the graph found only 0.33 and 0.8 of the runtime tests for
   294	   C01 and C02 (BM-7). That is why the full suite runs before committing.
   295	
   296	## Core requirements
   297	
   298	They serve the vision, `vision-statements/caches-memory-vision-statements.md`, and cover the two memory caches,
   299	full-text and vector; the code graph is its own project (`docs/code-graph/`). They are grouped by what happens to a
   300	cache: it is **built**, it is **kept** true, and it is **read** (EJ, 2026-10-12: *"let rewrite using these 3
   301	categories first"*). Each requirement names its source: **(v)** is the vision, **(d)** is this document's text, and
   302	*proposed* marks wording EJ has not decided. Each has a check, **Fails if**, that some case breaks. There are no fixed
   303	numbers: where a limit applies, it is a comparison that measurement decides. The ids R1–R9 they replace are given in
   304	brackets, because run records cite them.
   305	
   306	### Build — when a cache comes to exist, and what goes in it
   307	
   308	**B1 — Nothing is built before its trigger fires** (v: never built before its own trigger; was R5)
   309	Each cache has an observable trigger. This is the requirement that keeps the whole layer honest. The query-time
   310	counterpart — which store to reach for once a trigger has fired — is the section **When to use which store** above.
   311	
   312	- **B1.0 EJ decides** — when a cache's comparison passes, EJ decides whether it is built; a passed comparison alone
   313	  builds nothing (EJ, 2026-10-12: *"EJ decides"*; the 5W1H tables below say the same). The agent that told EJ
   314	  writes down his decision — build or not, his words, the date — beside the comparison result in `runs/<run-id>/`
   315	  (*proposed*; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes, write them in"*).
   316	- **B1.1 Full-text** — built only when, on a question set built for the comparison (Rd4.2), full-text reaches the right
   317	  entry after reading less than `INDEX.md` plus grep does, with no more misses. Measured as the code-and-docs benchmark
   318	  was (BM-1 to BM-9): the same questions through both routes, the characters read to the first right entry, and the
   319	  misses. Characters read is a proxy, not a token count, and a weak one: it stops at the right file, not the right
   320	  answer (**Measured here**, who wrote each check). A route answers a question when the right entry is in its top 5
   321	  results; a question it does not answer counts as a miss, never left out as the benchmark did (*"A miss is left out,
   322	  never scored 0"*, `bench/results.md`); characters are compared only over the questions both routes answered
   323	  (*proposed*). The benchmark had no `INDEX.md` arm; this comparison adds one. The comparison decides; no fixed size is
   324	  set in advance (S-F5: nothing is measured yet for a corpus of a few hundred to a few thousand short entries; S-F13
   325	  measured only speed, on synthetic entries). (was R5.1)
   326	  - **The comparison** runs on a trial index built in `runs/<run-id>/`, never in `project-memory/.cache/`
   327	    (*proposed*). It runs when EJ asks, or when an agent notes in its journal that `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read
   328	    through many hits to reach an entry; the agent that runs it writes the result there; if it passes, the agent tells
   329	    EJ, who decides (B1.0) (*proposed*; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes , made that change"*).
   330	- **B1.2 Vector** — built only when, on the misses noted in agents' journals (Rd4.1), the vector index (the hybrid
   331	  index, under the proposal below) finds an entry that full-text missed, and loses nothing full-text already found. (was
   332	  R5.2; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"ok write it into CACHES.md"*)
   333	  - **A miss** is a question where the entry existed, the asker's words share no term with it, `INDEX.md`, grep and
   334	    full-text search all missed it, and the agent found it another way (V-F13, the researcher's inference). The agent
   335	    that found it notes it in its journal (Rd4.1).
   336	  - **Finds** means the right entry is in the top 5 results (*proposed*; the benchmark's measure, BM-1 to BM-5).
   337	  - **Loses nothing** means: every question in the B1.1 question set that full-text answers is still answered, and the
   338	    characters read to the first right entry, over the questions both answered, are no more than full-text's
   339	    (*proposed*; the same measure as B1.1, with its weakness).
   340	  - **The comparison** runs on a trial index built in `runs/<run-id>/`, never in `project-memory/.cache/`
   341	    (*proposed*). It is rerun when an agent notes a miss, and when EJ asks; the agent that runs it writes the result
   342	    there; if it passes, the agent tells EJ, who decides (B1.0) (*proposed*).
   343	  - *Proposed* (hybrid only, **Vector index** why 5; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"label hybrid as proposed"*): the vector index
   344	    is combined with full-text, so it is built only after B1.1 has fired, and the comparison runs on the hybrid
   345	    index.
   346	  - **Fails if** a vector index exists in `project-memory/.cache/` with no recorded comparison in which it found at
   347	    least one missed entry and lost none of full-text's answers, or with no recorded decision by EJ.
   348	- **Fails if** a cache exists under `project-memory/.cache/` with no recorded comparison showing its trigger fired,
   349	  or with no recorded decision by EJ to build it; or a passed comparison has no record that EJ was told.
   350	
   351	**B2 — Derived from the files** (v: never the source of truth; was R1)
   352	The cache is computed from the memory's markdown and never holds knowledge of its own (M4, M6; M6 is marked
   353	CONTESTED in its source, where one engine proposed SQLite as a structured state store).
   354	
   355	- **B2.1** Deleting the cache and rebuilding from the same files restores every answer, provided the vector index is
   356	  rebuilt with the same embedding model and settings (a changed model makes every vector useless, V-F24).
   357	  *Example:* `rm -rf project-memory/.cache/` → run the documented rebuild → the same query returns the same entries.
   358	  (M6) (was R1.1)
   359	- **B2.2** The cache directory is ignored by git (`git check-ignore` reports it), and a rebuild adds nothing to
   360	  `git status --short` that was not there before it. (M6) (was R1.3)
   361	- **Fails if** the same query returns different entries after a delete and a rebuild from the same files with the
   362	  same embedding model, `git check-ignore` does not report the cache directory, or a rebuild adds a line to
   363	  `git status --short`.
   364	
   365	**B3 — Full rebuild, never patched** (v: never patched; was R2.1)
   366	- **B3.1** The documented rebuild regenerates the whole index from the files; no incremental update path exists.
   367	  *Example:* the rebuild command has no "update" mode. (G-F5 drift)
   368	- **Fails if** the rebuild has an update or incremental mode, or reuses any row or vector from the previous index.
   369	
   370	**B4 — Only active entries** (d: Rules for every cache, my reading; was R6)
   371	- **B4.1** The index holds every file in `entries/` whose own header says `status: active`, and nothing else — not
   372	  `journal/` or `work/` — each row carrying `covers`. Every row is active, so no row stores `status`
   373	  (*proposed*; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"others can accept and apply"*). The headers are the reference, not
   374	  `INDEX.md`, which is generated from them (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` K6.1) (*proposed*). *Example:* a superseded entry
   375	  never appears in a search result as if current. (V-F22, my reading)
   376	- **Fails if** the index holds a file whose header is not `status: active`, lacks an active entry, holds a file
   377	  outside `entries/`, or has a row without `covers`.
   378	
   379	**B5 — No model call to rebuild, except embedding** (v: How we know it works; was R8.1)
   380	- **B5.1** The documented rebuild completes without a model call, except the embedding step of B1.2.
   381	  *Example:* rebuild with no model credentials available; the full-text path still works. (M4, M6)
   382	- **Fails if** the full-text rebuild calls a model, or the vector rebuild calls any model but its embedding model.
   383	
   384	### Keep — the cache stays true to the files, and stays worth having
   385	
   386	**K1 — It records what it was built from** (d: Rules for every cache; was R2.2)
   387	- **K1.1** The index records a content hash of each entry file it was built from, so a stale one is detectable,
   388	  including edits not yet committed (*proposed*: a commit alone misses those). A newest-mtime check, the one `S-F9`
   389	  uses, is not enough: a deletion leaves no newer file (K2.1; my reading). (S-F11: SHA-256 on files)
   390	- **K1.2** The vector index also records the id of the embedding model it was built with, because a changed model
   391	  makes every vector useless (V-F24; the vector 5W1H stores it beside each vector).
   392	- **Fails if** the index carries no content hash of its sources, or the vector index carries no embedding-model id.
   393	
   394	**K2 — Stale never answers** (d: Rules for every cache; was R3.1)
   395	- **K2.1** Before answering, the tool compares the recorded content hash with the entry files, and for the vector
   396	  index the recorded model id with the one a rebuild would use now (*proposed*). If either differs it rebuilds,
   397	  building into a temporary file and renaming over the old one — so a deleted entry cannot stay searchable. (S-F9:
   398	  rebuild before answering; S-F11: the hash)
   399	- **K2.2** Only one rebuild runs at a time in a working copy: a second one waits for it, then compares the hash again
   400	  (K2.1) before answering. Each worktree has its own `.cache/`, because the folder is gitignored, so agents on
   401	  different worktrees never share one (*proposed*; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes, write them in"*).
   402	- **K2.3** The full-text and vector indexes are rebuilt by one command, in one step. If either fails, neither
   403	  replaces the old ones, and the query falls back (Rd3.5) (*proposed*; the vector 5W1H and V-F24: a second rebuild
   404	  step lets the two disagree).
   405	- **Fails if** after an entry is added, changed or deleted, or the embedding model changes, a query answers from the
   406	  old index; two rebuilds write to one cache at once; or the two indexes were built from different content hashes.
   407	
   408	**K3 — Dropped when it stops paying** (d; vision: *"cheap … and not before"*; was R8.2; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes please
   409	write"*)
   410	- **K3.1** A built cache is expected to keep passing its own trigger (K3.3 says what happens when it does not): its B1
   411	  comparison is rerun, on a question set built then and, for the vector index, on the noted misses (Rd4) (*proposed*;
   412	  the rebuild-cost part was dropped, EJ, 2026-10-12: *"drop the K3 cost part"*).
   413	- **K3.2** The comparison is rerun when an agent notes a miss, and when EJ asks; the agent that runs it writes the
   414	  result to `runs/<run-id>/` (*proposed*, the same as B1.2).
   415	- **K3.3** If the cache no longer passes, the agent tells EJ, who decides whether to drop it; his decision is recorded
   416	  as in B1.0. Dropping is deleting it; memory is unaffected (B2, Rd3) (*proposed*, mirroring B1.0).
   417	- **Fails if** a cache stays in `project-memory/.cache/` after a rerun it failed, with no recorded decision by EJ to
   418	  keep it.
   419	
   420	### Read — an agent queries it
   421	
   422	**Rd1 — Never read at session start** (v: never read at session start; was R9)
   423	- **Rd1.1** No cache file is part of what a session loads at start. *Example:* the session-start reads in
   424	  `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` (§ When it will be used) name no file under `project-memory/.cache/`; a start hook that
   425	  opens `.cache/index.sqlite` fails this. (M18; CP6, codex: *"Do not bulk-load … an entire search index at
   426	  startup"*)
   427	- **Fails if** a start hook or instruction opens any file under `project-memory/.cache/`.
   428	
   429	**Rd2 — An answer names its entry** (d; was R7)
   430	- **Rd2.1** Every result prints the entry file it came from, so the agent can open the source before acting on it
   431	  (M19: *"open the primary source when a retrieved statement will determine an action"*). *Example:* search output
   432	  names `entries/D-0012-*.md`. (*proposed*: "the agent", not "a human", per M19 and the vision; EJ, 2026-10-12:
   433	  *"write fixes 1–4 into CACHES.md"*)
   434	- **Fails if** a result does not name its entry file.
   435	
   436	**Rd3 — Absence degrades, it does not fail** (v: delete the folder and memory is unaffected; was R1.2, R3.2, R4)
   437	- **Rd3.1** Memory is fully readable with no cache present. *Example:* with `.cache/` absent, session start and an
   438	  on-demand `grep` over `entries/` both work. (M6) (was R1.2)
   439	- **Rd3.2** A missing cache file or a missing FTS5 falls back to grep, and every fallback answer says it came from
   440	  grep, not the cache, so the agent knows its results are unranked. (S-F9) (was R4.1; *proposed*: "says so in every
   441	  answer" replaces "warns once")
   442	- **Rd3.3** A missing embedding model falls back to full-text if it is there, and to grep if not; the answer says
   443	  so. *(my reading, extending S-F9; *proposed*: full-text before grep.)* (was R4.2)
   444	- **Rd3.4** A wrong path is never mistaken for an empty index: the cache is opened read-only and "no such file" is
   445	  treated as absent. *Example:* point the tool at a typo'd path → it falls back to grep and says so, it does not
   446	  create an empty database. (G-F5, failure mode 5) (was R3.2)
   447	- **Rd3.5** A rebuild that fails falls back to grep; it never answers from the stale index (K2) (*proposed*).
   448	- **Fails if** with the cache, FTS5 or the embedding model missing, the path mistyped, or a rebuild failing, a query
   449	  errors, answers from an empty or stale index, or answers from grep without saying so.
   450	
   451	**Rd4 — Misses are noted; question sets are built when needed** (*proposed*; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes , made that change"*)
   452	- **Rd4.1** When an agent finds an entry that `INDEX.md`, grep and full-text all missed (the definition in B1.2), it
   453	  notes the miss in its `journal/` file as it happens: the question, the search terms it used, the entry it found,
   454	  and how it found it. B1.2 and K3 run on these notes. Every other question goes unrecorded: a log of every search
   455	  costs every search, and the comparisons do not need it.
   456	- **Rd4.2** The question set for a comparison is built when the comparison runs: questions with known answers, written
   457	  from the entries that exist then and checked by a second agent, as the code-and-docs benchmark was (**Measured
   458	  here**: codex wrote the questions, five Claude agents checked them). The run records who wrote and who checked it.
   459	- **Fails if** an agent's `state.md` or journal describes finding an entry that search missed, with no journal note
   460	  holding the question, the terms and the entry; or a comparison runs on a question set with no record of who wrote
   461	  it and who checked it.
   462	
   463	## Example numbers by case
   464	
   465	The triggers in B1 and K3 are comparisons. The measurement decides them, so no number is fixed in advance. This
   466	section shows **what those comparisons have come out at elsewhere**, by agent type and condition, so a reader knows
   467	roughly where a project stands before measuring. These are examples with sources, not thresholds.
   468	
   469	Run: `runs/20261012-caches-numbers/`. Its source ids are:
   470	- `W-*`: web research (`nodes/web.md`);
   471	- `OC`: opencode's research (`nodes/opencode.md`);
   472	- `SZ`: the SEZA measurement (`nodes/seza.md`; every number with its command);
   473	- `CX`: codex's independent second look at SEZA, by agent type (`nodes/codex.md`);
   474	- `BM-*`, `S-F*`, `V-F*`, `G-F*`, `s3-F*`: as above.
   475	
   476	Web quotes are model-made extracts, not byte-checked, except W-1. Numbers marked *my arithmetic* are computed from a
   477	sourced figure.
   478	
   479	### The index read at session start (`INDEX.md`, `MEMORY.md`), by agent type
   480	
   481	| Agent | Window | Limit on files loaded at start | Measured effective context | Example |
   482	|---|---|---|---|---|
   483	| Claude Code (Claude 5.x) | 1M (W-8) | `MEMORY.md`: "first 200 lines … or the first 25KB, whichever comes first"; `CLAUDE.md`: target under 200 lines (W-1) | Opus 4.6, MRCR v2: 76% at 1M (W-14); 93% at 256K (W-15, reported) | 25 KB binds first: about 150 lines at 167 B per line (W-1), about 107 lines at SEZA's 233 B per line (*my arithmetic*, SZ) |
   484	| Codex (GPT-6.x) | 1.05M per the API (W-9), but this machine's Codex model cache says `context_window: 500000`, 95% effective, so about 475K (CX) | `AGENTS.md` combined up to 32 KiB (`project_doc_max_bytes`) (W-2) | GPT-5.4: 79.3% at 128K–256K, 36.6% at 512K–1M (W-15, reported) | instructions plus index under 32 KiB |
   485	| agy (Gemini 3.1 Pro / 3.8 Flash) | 1,048,576 (W-9, CX) | 24,000 bytes per rule file, **and** a 20,000-token budget across all rules (CX, official page); 12,000 characters in other reports (W-4, conflict) | 84.9% at 128K, 26.3% at 1M (W-15, reported) | under 12,000 characters if both readings must hold (*my reading*) |
   486	| opencode (MiniMax M3 / M2.7) | 1M / 204,800 (W-9) | no limit stated (W-6) | GAP | follow the strictest agent sharing the repo |
   487	| dsh (DeepSeek flash / V4 pro) | 1,048,576 (CX) | a 65,536-byte budget for the instruction files it loads (CX, from its loader docs) | GAP | instructions plus index under 64 KiB |
   488	| Qwen Code | — | its auto-memory index is capped at 200 lines or 25,000 bytes (`MAX_MANAGED_AUTO_MEMORY_INDEX_LINES` / `_BYTES`, CX); it also warns when always-loaded context passes 15% of the window, an advisory only (CX) | — | the index cap binds first: under 25,000 bytes, as for Claude |
   489	| Cursor | — | rules "under 500 lines" (W-3) | — | — |
   490	
   491	**Why the start load should stay small, whatever the window:**
   492	- Half of 17 models kept satisfactory performance only up to 32K (RULER, W-10).
   493	- 11 models fell below 50% of their baseline at 32K when the question and the answer share few words (NoLiMa, W-11).
   494	- Focused prompts of about 300 tokens beat full ones of about 113K (W-13).
   495	- Context files raised inference cost by over 20%, with no general success gain (W-17).
   496	- At 500 simultaneous instructions, the best model was 68% accurate (W-16).
   497	
   498	**Use case, SEZA (SZ, CX):**
   499	- The start load by agent, as estimates: Claude Code 61,993 B (about 15.4K tokens, including the global `CLAUDE.md`;
   500	  SZ measured 58,427 B without it); dsh 46,319 B (about 11.5K); Codex and agy 29,166 B each (about 7.2K) (CX). All are
   501	  well under 15% of every window above.
   502	- `MEMORY.md` is 52 lines and 12,108 B, within Claude's limit.
   503	- The 52 topic files (159,524 B) would not fit in 25 KB. That is why they are an index plus files read on demand.
   504	
   505	**When several agents share a project, the binding limit is the smallest one among them** (*my reading*).
   506	
   507	### B1.1: full-text against `INDEX.md` plus grep
   508	
   509	| Case | What was measured | Source |
   510	|---|---|---|
   511	| 1,144 docs, 1.7M tokens | the grep agent is more accurate (77.4 vs 74.7) but reads 226K tokens per question against BM25's 5.8K | W-22 |
   512	| 6,980 docs, 8.5M tokens | accuracy crossover: BM25 70.1 vs 69.9; BM25 ahead at every larger tier | W-22, S-F4 |
   513	| 511,959 docs, 600.8M tokens | BM25 50.5 vs the grep agent 30.7 | W-22 |
   514	| This repo's code and docs, 490 files, 4.8 MB | full-text prints about a sixth of grep's characters before naming a right file | BM-6 |
   515	| SEZA memory: the Claude memory folder (55 files) and a wider set of 257 files (2.5 MB) | grep takes 4–7 ms but prints from 361 B (a narrow term, memory folder) to 109,519 B, about 27K tokens (a broad term, 257-file set). Over the 257 files, a full-text proxy ranked the right file first on 3 of 5 questions and missed on 2 (n=5, one reader) | SZ |
   516	| A tool result too large to read | filtering in code instead of reading: 150,000 tokens down to 2,000 | OC (Anthropic, 2025-11-04) |
   517	
   518	**My reading:** on accuracy, grep stays competitive until millions of tokens. On **tokens read**, full-text wins much
   519	earlier: on SEZA-sized memory, a single broad term already floods grep's output. The B1.1 comparison measures exactly
   520	that.
   521	
   522	### B1.2: the vector index
   523	
   524	| Case | What was measured | Source |
   525	|---|---|---|
   526	| Loading about 115K tokens of history instead of retrieving | 30%–55% lower accuracy (GPT-4o, Llama 3.1) | W-18 |
   527	| Long context against retrieval, 100K–1M tokens | retrieval better for 3 of 4 models at 100K, and for all 4 from 500K | W-19 |
   528	| Vendor guidance | under about 200,000 tokens, the whole base can go in the prompt | V-F8 (2024) |
   529	| Dense against BM25, 1,144–511,959 docs | dense lowest at every tier in the table (7 tiers) | W-22 |
   530	| Enterprise corpus, 500K docs | vector 51.4 vs BM25 68.8 correctness | V-F4 |
   531	| Code, 1,000 or more files | +12.5% accuracy with vector search (Cursor), but 15 days later Cursor shipped Instant Grep and says it "does not store embeddings of your codebase for search" | V-F6, OC |
   532	| A memory growing 10× (1M to 10M tokens) | Mem0 64.1 to 48.6 (vendor's figures) | W-20 |
   533	| SEZA | no recorded misses, so the comparison has nothing to run on | SZ |
   534	
   535	**My reading:** retrieval beats loading everything somewhere around 100K–500K tokens, but it is keyword retrieval
   536	that wins. Vectors earn a place only on recorded meaning-misses, which is what B1.2 tests.

     1	# Caches — product vision statement
     2	
     3	> **Status: settled** (accepted by EJ on 2026-10-12, and again after the changes below: *"others can accept and
     4	> apply"*). EJ's first words: *"ok the vision is confirm"*. Drafted 2026-10-12 by the COO from the research in
     5	> `runs/20261012-memory-caches/nodes/` and `runs/20261011-project-memory-engines/RECOMMENDATION.md`; rewritten the same
     6	> day at EJ's direction — more direct, fewer hedges.
     7	>
     8	> **Drawn from:** `docs/memory-system/CACHES.md`, which carries the core requirements (Build / Keep / Read) this
     9	> vision serves. This file holds the vision only, so each has one copy (`docs/workflow-templates/blueprint.md` keeps
    10	> vision and requirements in separate section files).
    11	>
    12	> **Whose it is.** Per the blueprint template, the product vision is EJ's — *"an agent may only write down what EJ
    13	> has said"*. This was drafted from the research at his request and settled by him on 2026-10-12.
    14	>
    15	> **Changed 2026-10-12:** the code graph removed at EJ's direction (*"code graph should have it's own project"*); its
    16	> draft is `docs/code-graph/CODE_GRAPH.md`. The problem now names both ways it shows up, one per cache — the vector
    17	> reason was missing (EJ: *"ok write it into the vision"*). "How we know it works" now tests that the right entry is
    18	> found for less (B1.1, B1.2), not only that a rebuild repeats; and the rebuild's "no model call" now excepts the vector
    19	> embedding step, as B5.1 does (EJ: *"ok write it into the vision"*). "Too many entries" no longer says `INDEX.md` stops
    20	> listing every active entry: the full-text trigger is the B1.1 comparison (EJ: *"the comparison for full-text"*), and
    21	> `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` requires `INDEX.md` to list every active entry and still fit (K6.1, Rd3.1). Then, on acceptance
    22	> (EJ, 2026-10-12: *"others can accept and apply"*): "who decides" replaces "who sets the triggers"; the grep timing is
    23	> one labelled figure; `G-F5` is labelled as a code-graph study; and "never answers from a stale index" is added. The
    24	> tests run on "the project's own questions and noted misses": the full question log was dropped (EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes
    25	> , made that change"*).
    26	
    27	The caches are two optional indexes over the project's markdown memory — full-text search and vector search. Both
    28	live in `project-memory/.cache/`, both are gitignored, and both are rebuilt from the files. Neither is needed today.
    29	
    30	**Who it is for.** An agent retrieving an entry on demand. Not the human reviewer, who reads the entries themselves,
    31	and not EJ, who decides whether a cache is built once its comparison passes (B1.0).
    32	
    33	**The problem it solves.** The memory is markdown in git, and today `INDEX.md` plus grep finds what an agent needs — in
    34	the researcher's own test, a one-word grep over 10,000 synthetic entries took 0.038 s (`S-F13`). As the memory grows,
    35	that stops being enough in two ways:
    36	- **Too many entries — full-text search.** Reaching the right entry through `INDEX.md` and grep means reading more
    37	  and more: grep returns unranked matches, and every hit an agent opens costs context (`S-F1`).
    38	- **Different words — vector search.** The agent's words differ from the entry's, so `INDEX.md`, grep and
    39	  full-text search all miss an entry that exists (`V-F13`).
    40	
    41	The caches exist to keep finding the right entry cheap when that happens — and not before.
    42	
    43	**What it will never do.**
    44	- It is never the source of truth. The markdown is, always, and a cache holds nothing the files do not (`M4`, `M6`).
    45	- It is never patched incrementally. It is rebuilt whole or not at all — a live, incrementally synced index drifted
    46	  4.3% of its edges from a clean rebuild with every integrity check passing (`G-F5`: a study of a code-graph tool,
    47	  kept as evidence of how a patched index fails).
    48	- It never answers from a stale index. Before answering, it is checked against the files and rebuilt if they
    49	  differ (K2) — stale state, not search, is what went wrong in SEZA (`CACHES.md` § SEZA today).
    50	- It is never read at session start (`M18`).
    51	- It is never built before its own trigger fires. Every trigger is an observable comparison, and the measurement
    52	  decides it, not a number set in advance (EJ, 2026-10-12: *"this is design guide, how can I give you a number?"*).
    53	- It never becomes a second thing to keep in sync: delete the folder and memory is unaffected.
    54	
    55	**How we know it works.** Two tests, on the project's own questions and noted misses.
    56	- **It finds the right entry, for less.** Full-text search reaches the right entry after reading less than
    57	  `INDEX.md` plus grep, with no more misses (B1.1). Vector search, run on the recorded misses, finds entries
    58	  full-text did not, and does no worse on the other questions (B1.2).
    59	- **It holds nothing of its own.** Delete the cache, rebuild it from the files, and the same query returns the
    60	  same entries, with no model call except the vector embedding step (`M6`, B5.1; a 10,000-entry FTS rebuild takes
    61	  0.53 s, `S-F13`).
    62	
    63	One caveat travels with every number here: none of it has been measured on a real `project-memory/` corpus,
    64	because that corpus does not exist yet (`S-F5`, `V-F11`).

-## Rules for all three caches
+## Rules for every cache
 
 These apply to whichever cache is built. They come from how the researched tools fail.
 
 - **Full rebuild, never patched.** A live, incrementally updated index drifted silently from a clean rebuild: 4.3% of
   edges wrong, with every integrity check passing (`G-F5 drift`). Its makers decided drift cannot be measured cheaply
-  (`G-F5c`). A full rebuild of this repo's code graph takes 0.94 s (`G-F12`); the benchmark's own code-graph rebuild
-  takes 0.89 s (BM-9); an FTS rebuild of 10,000 synthetic entries takes 0.53 s (`S-F13`).
+  (`G-F5c`). An FTS rebuild of 10,000 synthetic entries takes 0.53 s (`S-F13`).
 - **Rebuild before answering when stale.** Before each query, compare the recorded content hash of the
   sources with the index and rebuild if they differ. Build into a temporary file and rename it over the old one
   (`S-F9`, quoted: *"A stale index is worse than no index."*).

-## Code graph
-
-### Five whys
-
-1. **Why a code graph?** To answer structural questions about the code, such as what an edit touches.
-2. **Why would memory need that?** For "which entries cover the files I am editing" (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` Rd4.1). The
-   drift check it once also served was removed by EJ on 2026-10-12.
-3. **Why is a text search enough?** `covers:` names **paths**, not symbols, and is already a hand-kept index from entry
-   to path (`G-F14`), so finding the entries that cover a file is a search over `covers:` lines.
-4. **Why not add a graph anyway?** An incrementally synced graph adds a silent failure that a text search does not have
-   (`G-F5 drift`, 4.3% of edges wrong). A full-rebuild graph such as the repo's own does not drift (`G-F18`), but it
-   covers Python only, so for other `covers:` paths it gives a silent partial answer (code-graph node §5, option B).
-   Whether an entry is still true is answered by reading it against the code (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` Rd5), not by a
-   graph.
-5. **Why might it be needed later?** Either of two conditions in the code-graph node's §6, each a test:
-   - `covers:` starts naming functions or classes instead of files;
-   - an agent must answer about files not on any `covers:` list ("what does this change affect").
-
-### 5W1H
-
-| | |
-|---|---|
-| **Who** | Any agent, by a text search over `covers:` lines. EJ decides to build a graph if one of the two conditions holds. |
-| **What** | Today: no graph for memory. A text search over `covers:` lines. If a condition holds: the repo's own `code_graph/code_graph.py` (Python only, deterministic, full rebuild, no incremental mode, `G-F18`) before any outside tool. |
-| **When** | Today: before editing a file (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` Rd4.1). A graph, if ever built, is rebuilt in full each time it is used. |
-| **Where** | Nothing today. Later, `project-memory/.cache/code-graph.json`, gitignored. |
-| **Why** | The five whys above. |
-| **How** | `rg` for the file's path in the `covers:` lines of `entries/`. External indexers (codegraph) are not adopted: their win is measured on source-code architecture questions, not on memory (`G-F7`, `G-F14`), and they bring the drift in `G-F5 drift`. |

   229	## Measured here: development and testing
   230	
   231	EJ's aim is that development and testing get faster and cheaper in tokens. So this section measures all three stores
   232	against grep on **this repo**: 490 files (123 `.py`, 367 `.md`, 4.8 MB), with 40 questions an agent asks while
   233	developing and testing it. Run: `runs/20261012-caches-routing/` (results in `bench/results.md`, ids `BM-*` below).
   234	
   235	**Who wrote each check, and what it does not establish.**
   236	- The **questions and answers** were written by codex (`gpt-6.1-sol`), each checked by an `rg` command it ran. Five
   237	  Claude agents re-checked all 40: 37 were correct and 3 were incomplete. Those 3 were completed.
   238	- **"Which tests run this code"** is also scored against per-test runtime coverage (`pytest-cov`, no model). That
   239	  counts in-process Python only, so it misses tests that run the code as a separate process.
   240	- The **harness** was written by opencode. A Claude audit found 5 high-severity biases, and a Claude repair fixed
   241	  them; the repair was **not re-audited**. Rebuilding from scratch gave the same answers on all 180 question-route
   242	  pairs.
   243	- **Token cost** is measured as *characters printed up to the first line that names a right file*. That is a proxy,
   244	  not a token count, and a weak one:
   245	  - it stops at the right *file*, not the right answer;
   246	  - misses are left out, so each route's mean is over a different set of questions;
   247	  - each route prints a different format.
   248	
   249	  No agent was run end to end.
   250	- One repo, one question set, **n = 6 to 10 per category**. None of this is a project-memory corpus.
   251	
   252	**What each route returns, per category** (symbol hit in the top 5 / characters read to the first line that names a
   253	right file):
   254	
   255	| | grep (`rg`) | SQLite full-text | vector | hybrid | code graph |
   256	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
   257	| **BM-1** exact identifier (8) | 0.75 / 920 | 0.63 / 356 | 0.0 / 837 | 0.5 / 91 | **0.75 / 43** |
   258	| **BM-2** concept in other words (8) | 0 / — | 0 / — | 0 (file 0.25) / 955 | 0 (file 0.13) / 1,427 | n/a |
   259	| **BM-3** structure and test impact (10) | 0.8 / 1,015 | **0.9 / 175** | 0.1 / 1,421 | 0.9 / 362 | 0.7 / **61** |
   260	| **BM-4** decision or reason in the docs (8) | 0.63 / 4,470 | **0.63 / 525** | 0.5 / 810 | 0.63 / 516 | 0.0 (over 2 questions) |
   261	| **BM-5** how to run or configure (6) | 0.17 / 6,875 | 0.33 / 1,146 | 0.5 / 569 | 0.5 / 1,120 | n/a |
   262	
   263	- **BM-6, all categories.** Characters printed up to the first right file (each mean over that route's hits only:
   264	  grep 26, full-text 25, vector 19, hybrid 26, graph 13): grep 2,688, full-text 451, hybrid 528, vector
   265	  923, graph 53 (graph over the 20 questions that name a symbol or file). Total output per answer: grep 4,304,
   266	  full-text 1,817.
   267	- **BM-7, test impact against runtime coverage** (C01–C04, n=4). The macro-average test-file recall of the tests that
   268	  really run the function each route found: graph 0.78; grep, full-text and hybrid 0.59; vector 0.04.
   269	- **BM-8, tests that run the code as a separate process** (C05–C07). The graph returns **nothing**, because no
   270	  call edge exists. Full-text search on the file name found 2 of 3; grep named a gold file on 2 of 3 and the
   271	  complete set on 1 of 3, printing up to 8.7K
   272	  characters.
   273	- **BM-9, speed.** Every route answers in at most 15 ms per question; the slowest was grep on E04, at 14.7 ms. Full
   274	  rebuilds take 0.17 s for full-text (9.5 MB), 0.94 s for vector (10.2 MB) and 0.89 s for the graph (4.9 MB); the
   275	  graph's own rebuild for `G-F12` was measured separately at 0.94 s. The full test suite, 529 tests, takes 31–33 s (3
   276	  runs).
   277	
   278	**What the numbers say (my reading).**
   279	1. **On this repo, speed is not the reason to use a cache.** Every route takes milliseconds (BM-9). The difference is
   280	   in **what the route prints**: full-text prints about a sixth of grep's characters before naming a right file
   281	   (BM-6). Whether that becomes a sixth of an agent's tokens is not measured. Where
   282	   the question names a symbol or file, the graph gets there in tens of characters (BM-1, BM-3); its BM-6 mean is over
   283	   those 20 questions only, so it is not a like-for-like ratio.
   284	2. **The vector index loses almost everywhere it was tried.** It never found an exact identifier (BM-1), and its
   285	   test-file recall against runtime coverage was 0.04 (BM-7). Its only edge is "how do I run or configure X" (BM-5,
   286	   n=6), which is
   287	   too small to act on. Nothing here contradicts the earlier rule that vectors come last and only as a hybrid.
   288	3. **No route answers a concept asked in other words in one shot** (BM-2). That still takes an agent searching and
   289	   reading. It is exactly the miss the vector trigger is meant to record.
   290	4. **No single store answers "which tests should I run".** The graph finds tests that reach the code indirectly
   291	   (BM-7), but is blind to tests that run it as a separate process (BM-8). Full-text search on the file name finds
   292	   the latter. An empty graph answer means *not seen*, never *no tests*. Even combined, the two routes
   293	   can miss affected tests: both missed C05 entirely, and the graph found only 0.33 and 0.8 of the runtime tests for
   294	   C01 and C02 (BM-7). That is why the full suite runs before committing.
   295	
   296	## Core requirements
   297	
   298	They serve the vision, `vision-statements/caches-memory-vision-statements.md`, and cover the two memory caches,
   299	full-text and vector; the code graph is its own project (`docs/code-graph/`). They are grouped by what happens to a
   300	cache: it is **built**, it is **kept** true, and it is **read** (EJ, 2026-10-12: *"let rewrite using these 3
   301	categories first"*). Each requirement names its source: **(v)** is the vision, **(d)** is this document's text, and
   302	*proposed* marks wording EJ has not decided. Each has a check, **Fails if**, that some case breaks. There are no fixed
   303	numbers: where a limit applies, it is a comparison that measurement decides. The ids R1–R9 they replace are given in
   304	brackets, because run records cite them.
   305	
   306	### Build — when a cache comes to exist, and what goes in it
   307	
   308	**B1 — Nothing is built before its trigger fires** (v: never built before its own trigger; was R5)
   309	Each cache has an observable trigger. This is the requirement that keeps the whole layer honest. The query-time
   310	counterpart — which store to reach for once a trigger has fired — is the section **When to use which store** above.
   311	
   312	- **B1.0 EJ decides** — when a cache's comparison passes, EJ decides whether it is built; a passed comparison alone
   313	  builds nothing (EJ, 2026-10-12: *"EJ decides"*; the 5W1H tables below say the same). The agent that told EJ
   314	  writes down his decision — build or not, his words, the date — beside the comparison result in `runs/<run-id>/`
   315	  (*proposed*; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes, write them in"*).
   316	- **B1.1 Full-text** — built only when, on a question set built for the comparison (Rd4.2), full-text reaches the right
   317	  entry after reading less than `INDEX.md` plus grep does, with no more misses. Measured as the code-and-docs benchmark
   318	  was (BM-1 to BM-9): the same questions through both routes, the characters read to the first right entry, and the
   319	  misses. Characters read is a proxy, not a token count, and a weak one: it stops at the right file, not the right
   320	  answer (**Measured here**, who wrote each check). A route answers a question when the right entry is in its top 5
   321	  results; a question it does not answer counts as a miss, never left out as the benchmark did (*"A miss is left out,
   322	  never scored 0"*, `bench/results.md`); characters are compared only over the questions both routes answered
   323	  (*proposed*). The benchmark had no `INDEX.md` arm; this comparison adds one. The comparison decides; no fixed size is
   324	  set in advance (S-F5: nothing is measured yet for a corpus of a few hundred to a few thousand short entries; S-F13
   325	  measured only speed, on synthetic entries). (was R5.1)
   326	  - **The comparison** runs on a trial index built in `runs/<run-id>/`, never in `project-memory/.cache/`
   327	    (*proposed*). It runs when EJ asks, or when an agent notes in its journal that `INDEX.md` plus grep made it read
   328	    through many hits to reach an entry; the agent that runs it writes the result there; if it passes, the agent tells
   329	    EJ, who decides (B1.0) (*proposed*; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes , made that change"*).
   330	- **B1.2 Vector** — built only when, on the misses noted in agents' journals (Rd4.1), the vector index (the hybrid
   331	  index, under the proposal below) finds an entry that full-text missed, and loses nothing full-text already found. (was
   332	  R5.2; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"ok write it into CACHES.md"*)
   333	  - **A miss** is a question where the entry existed, the asker's words share no term with it, `INDEX.md`, grep and
   334	    full-text search all missed it, and the agent found it another way (V-F13, the researcher's inference). The agent
   335	    that found it notes it in its journal (Rd4.1).
   336	  - **Finds** means the right entry is in the top 5 results (*proposed*; the benchmark's measure, BM-1 to BM-5).
   337	  - **Loses nothing** means: every question in the B1.1 question set that full-text answers is still answered, and the
   338	    characters read to the first right entry, over the questions both answered, are no more than full-text's
   339	    (*proposed*; the same measure as B1.1, with its weakness).
   340	  - **The comparison** runs on a trial index built in `runs/<run-id>/`, never in `project-memory/.cache/`
   341	    (*proposed*). It is rerun when an agent notes a miss, and when EJ asks; the agent that runs it writes the result
   342	    there; if it passes, the agent tells EJ, who decides (B1.0) (*proposed*).
   343	  - *Proposed* (hybrid only, **Vector index** why 5; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"label hybrid as proposed"*): the vector index
   344	    is combined with full-text, so it is built only after B1.1 has fired, and the comparison runs on the hybrid
   345	    index.
   346	  - **Fails if** a vector index exists in `project-memory/.cache/` with no recorded comparison in which it found at
   347	    least one missed entry and lost none of full-text's answers, or with no recorded decision by EJ.
   348	- **Fails if** a cache exists under `project-memory/.cache/` with no recorded comparison showing its trigger fired,
   349	  or with no recorded decision by EJ to build it; or a passed comparison has no record that EJ was told.
   350	
   351	**B2 — Derived from the files** (v: never the source of truth; was R1)
   352	The cache is computed from the memory's markdown and never holds knowledge of its own (M4, M6; M6 is marked
   353	CONTESTED in its source, where one engine proposed SQLite as a structured state store).
   354	
   355	- **B2.1** Deleting the cache and rebuilding from the same files restores every answer, provided the vector index is
   356	  rebuilt with the same embedding model and settings (a changed model makes every vector useless, V-F24).
   357	  *Example:* `rm -rf project-memory/.cache/` → run the documented rebuild → the same query returns the same entries.
   358	  (M6) (was R1.1)
   359	- **B2.2** The cache directory is ignored by git (`git check-ignore` reports it), and a rebuild adds nothing to
   360	  `git status --short` that was not there before it. (M6) (was R1.3)
   361	- **Fails if** the same query returns different entries after a delete and a rebuild from the same files with the
   362	  same embedding model, `git check-ignore` does not report the cache directory, or a rebuild adds a line to
   363	  `git status --short`.
   364	
   365	**B3 — Full rebuild, never patched** (v: never patched; was R2.1)
   366	- **B3.1** The documented rebuild regenerates the whole index from the files; no incremental update path exists.
   367	  *Example:* the rebuild command has no "update" mode. (G-F5 drift)
   368	- **Fails if** the rebuild has an update or incremental mode, or reuses any row or vector from the previous index.
   369	
   370	**B4 — Only active entries** (d: Rules for every cache, my reading; was R6)
   371	- **B4.1** The index holds every file in `entries/` whose own header says `status: active`, and nothing else — not
   372	  `journal/` or `work/` — each row carrying `covers`. Every row is active, so no row stores `status`
   373	  (*proposed*; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"others can accept and apply"*). The headers are the reference, not
   374	  `INDEX.md`, which is generated from them (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` K6.1) (*proposed*). *Example:* a superseded entry
   375	  never appears in a search result as if current. (V-F22, my reading)
   376	- **Fails if** the index holds a file whose header is not `status: active`, lacks an active entry, holds a file
   377	  outside `entries/`, or has a row without `covers`.
   378	
   379	**B5 — No model call to rebuild, except embedding** (v: How we know it works; was R8.1)
   380	- **B5.1** The documented rebuild completes without a model call, except the embedding step of B1.2.
   381	  *Example:* rebuild with no model credentials available; the full-text path still works. (M4, M6)
   382	- **Fails if** the full-text rebuild calls a model, or the vector rebuild calls any model but its embedding model.
   383	
   384	### Keep — the cache stays true to the files, and stays worth having
   385	
   386	**K1 — It records what it was built from** (d: Rules for every cache; was R2.2)
   387	- **K1.1** The index records a content hash of each entry file it was built from, so a stale one is detectable,
   388	  including edits not yet committed (*proposed*: a commit alone misses those). A newest-mtime check, the one `S-F9`
   389	  uses, is not enough: a deletion leaves no newer file (K2.1; my reading). (S-F11: SHA-256 on files)
   390	- **K1.2** The vector index also records the id of the embedding model it was built with, because a changed model
   391	  makes every vector useless (V-F24; the vector 5W1H stores it beside each vector).
   392	- **Fails if** the index carries no content hash of its sources, or the vector index carries no embedding-model id.
   393	
   394	**K2 — Stale never answers** (d: Rules for every cache; was R3.1)
   395	- **K2.1** Before answering, the tool compares the recorded content hash with the entry files, and for the vector
   396	  index the recorded model id with the one a rebuild would use now (*proposed*). If either differs it rebuilds,
   397	  building into a temporary file and renaming over the old one — so a deleted entry cannot stay searchable. (S-F9:
   398	  rebuild before answering; S-F11: the hash)
   399	- **K2.2** Only one rebuild runs at a time in a working copy: a second one waits for it, then compares the hash again
   400	  (K2.1) before answering. Each worktree has its own `.cache/`, because the folder is gitignored, so agents on
   401	  different worktrees never share one (*proposed*; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes, write them in"*).
   402	- **K2.3** The full-text and vector indexes are rebuilt by one command, in one step. If either fails, neither
   403	  replaces the old ones, and the query falls back (Rd3.5) (*proposed*; the vector 5W1H and V-F24: a second rebuild
   404	  step lets the two disagree).
   405	- **Fails if** after an entry is added, changed or deleted, or the embedding model changes, a query answers from the
   406	  old index; two rebuilds write to one cache at once; or the two indexes were built from different content hashes.
   407	
   408	**K3 — Dropped when it stops paying** (d; vision: *"cheap … and not before"*; was R8.2; EJ, 2026-10-12: *"yes please
   409	write"*)
   410	- **K3.1** A built cache is expected to keep passing its own trigger (K3.3 says what happens when it does not): its B1
   411	  comparison is rerun, on a question set built then and, for the vector index, on the noted misses (Rd4) (*proposed*;
   412	  the rebuild-cost part was dropped, EJ, 2026-10-12: *"drop the K3 cost part"*).
   413	- **K3.2** The comparison is rerun when an agent notes a miss, and when EJ asks; the agent that runs it writes the
   414	  result to `runs/<run-id>/` (*proposed*, the same as B1.2).
   415	- **K3.3** If the cache no longer passes, the agent tells EJ, who decides whether to drop it; his decision is recorded
   416	  as in B1.0. Dropping is deleting it; memory is unaffected (B2, Rd3) (*proposed*, mirroring B1.0).
   417	- **Fails if** a cache stays in `project-memory/.cache/` after a rerun it failed, with no recorded decision by EJ to
   418	  keep it.
   419

The fallback mismatch and the conflict over B1.1 journal notes are supported by the current text. Some other findings look overstated: the benchmark IDs are introduced as labels “below,” and the joint-rebuild clause needs to be read with the rule that applies to whichever cache is built. I’m checking the remaining evidence claims before deciding what to hold or withdraw.

   136	- **D1 — What answers a free-text memory question?** opencode: **FTS5 first**, when `INDEX.md` is over capacity and
   137	  ranking matters, with vector only for meaning (`S-F1`, `S-F4`, `S-F3`). codex: **baseline first** — `INDEX.md` and
   138	  grep — then vector "alongside keyword search" when wording mismatch defeats the baseline; codex names **no FTS5**
   139	  anywhere (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). claude: **no store at all** for any memory question (`G-F2`, `G-F3`, `G-F10`,
   140	  `M17`, `M19`). **unresolved.**
   141	- **D2 — Is the FTS5/vector benefit measured?** opencode presents FTS5 against grep as a material token saving
   142	  (~5.8K vs ~226K per question; `S-F4`, `S-F13`). codex calls the vector benefit *"guidance, not a measured advantage
   143	  here"* (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`) and says its synthetic timings *"do not establish vector savings."* claude says
   144	  memory-corpus costs are GAP and there is no head-to-head (`S-F5`, `V-F11`, `G-F10`). **unresolved** — whether
   145	  `S-F4`'s 1,144-document BM25 study transfers to this project's 10,000-entry claim is not settled by the three files.

   147	  claude: **GAP per question**, "because it depends on hits and grep is unranked". codex: **tokens were not
   148	  measured** (`S-F13`). **unresolved.**
   149	- **D4 — Cost of `INDEX.md` at query time.** claude: **no extra read**, it is loaded at session start (`M17`).
   150	  opencode: **<0.01 s** scan, minimal header-only tokens (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`). codex: **time GAP and tokens GAP**
   151	  (`M17`, `M19`). Two engines read the same `M17` differently. **unresolved.**
   152	- **D5 — Does `INDEX.md` carry `covers:` (and status) per line?** codex asserts the `covers:` match directly
   153	  (`V-F21`). opencode asserts status per line (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:100`). claude hedges: `RECOMMENDATION.md`'s Layout
   154	  says the lines carry `covers`, `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` says only *"one line per active entry"* — so if `covers` is not
   155	  there, that row falls back to grep over `entries/`. **unresolved** (a documentation conflict; the three files do

   119	## Where it is put
   120

   122

   125	project-memory/

   127	  entries/           one file per entry: D- decision, F- fact, L- lesson, R- runbook
   128	  work/<task-id>/    state.md (the handoff), evidence/, journal/ (the task's raw notes, one file per agent session)
   129	  journal/           project raw notes, for work not tied to a task, one file per agent session; never loaded
   130	                     automatically
   131	  .cache/            optional search index, gitignored, rebuilt from the files

   264	
   265	**K5 — The journal becomes entries** (v: nothing lost between sessions)
   266	- **K5.1** Task and project memory each keep a journal: a task's in `work/<task-id>/journal/`, the project's in
   267	  `journal/` for work not tied to a task. Both hold raw notes, one file per agent session, appended to and never
   268	  edited. Agent and user memory keep no journal under this design (EJ, 2026-10-12).
   269	- **K5.2** At the end of a task, and before a handoff or session end, an agent reads its task's journal (or the project
   270	  journal) written since the last read, writes what is worth keeping as entries, and logs that read in the task's `state.md`. A session cut off
   271	  before it can read loses nothing: the next read covers it (EJ, 2026-10-12: no separate milestones, the start and the
   272	  end already cover them).
   273	- **Fails if** a task's `state.md` shows it complete or handed off with no journal-read log line.
   274	
   275	**K6 — `INDEX.md` stays in step** (d: Where it is put; When to write)
   276	- **K6.1** `INDEX.md` is generated from `entries/`: one line per active entry, showing its id, kind and one-line
   277	  claim. It is never edited by hand.
   278	- **K6.2** It is regenerated right after any decision or fact is written, so the next session sees it.
   279	- **Open:** which tool generates it and what runs it (a build detail).
   280	- **Fails if** regenerating `INDEX.md` changes it, meaning it was out of step, or it lists a superseded entry as
   281	  active.

   283	### Read — the right memory reaches the agent at the right time
   284	
   285	**Rd1 — Where it lives, and readable from a clone alone** (v: plain files; never a database or service)
   286	- **Rd1.1** `AGENTS.md` sits at the repo root (`CLAUDE.md` only imports it). `project-memory/` holds `INDEX.md`,
   287	  `entries/`, `work/<task-id>/` (with `state.md`, `evidence/` and `journal/`), `journal/`, and `.cache/`, which is

   289	- **Rd1.2** With no network, no cache and no private agent store, a fresh clone can read `INDEX.md` and every entry.

    99	Contextual Retrieval", 2024-09-19: "While embedding models excel at capturing semantic relationships, they can miss
   100	crucial exact matches"; BM25 "is particularly effective for queries that include unique identifiers or technical terms";
   101	top-20 failure rate 5.7% with embeddings alone, 2.9% with contextual embeddings plus contextual BM25, 1.9% with reranking.
   102	Datasets include codebases. Note this is a 2024 chunk-RAG test with API embeddings, and the baseline 5.7% is a
   103	chunk-level failure rate, not an agent-task result. URL: https://www.anthropic.com/news/contextual-retrieval.
   104

   106	tokens (about 500 pages of material), you can just include the entire knowledge base in the prompt that you give the

   170	### Q3. Current tools that build such an index from files that stay the source of truth
